the suite budget was consumed looping on i/o timeout and all subsequent tests received HTTP 401 Unauthorized as the in-flight service-account token expired during the outage. - **Breaking change:** Unknown - **Flake?** Yes - **Fix:** Re-run; if recurrent, audit kube-apiserver VIP availability 2026-07-02 07:31–08:31 UTC and verify Prow kubeconfig token TTL spans the full suite runtime. ### 8/21 failures — linuxptp-daemon log stream disconnect (tgm cascade) - **Suites:** dualfollower, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha | **Evidence:** PROVEN (cascade from tgm suite timeout at ~06:13 UTC) - **What:** tgm suite timeout aborted AfterSuite cleanup; downstream suites held stale pod references and saw `context deadline exceeded` on log stream reads as daemon pods recycled across 5 nodes. - **Breaking change:** Unknown - **Flake?** Yes — timing-sensitive; tgm timeout amplifies risk - **Fix:** Add a post-suite readiness gate waiting for all linuxptp-daemon pods Running/Ready before the next suite starts. ### 2/21 failures — T-GM holdover state transition failure - **Suites:** tgm | **Evidence:** PROVEN - **What:** T-GM never transitioned to clock class 7 (holdover) after simulated GNSS signal loss; the stalled holdover test exhausted the suite budget and starved the ts2phc recovery test entirely. - **Breaking change:** Unknown (no candidate PR identified; possible environment or regression) - **Flake?** Not determined - **Fix:** Investigate T-GM holdover config and GNSS signal simulation; --- ## Cross-Suite Summary — periodic-ci-openshift-release-main-nightly-4.22-e2e-telco5g-ptp **Version:** 4.22 | **Total failures:** 14 | **Suites:** bc, dualfollower, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha, oc, tbc, tgm **TL;DR:** A `main`-branch test regression (commit `d20ffcbf`) broke OS-clock-sync event parsing for 5 suites; T-BC and T-GM hardware-sync failures account for the remaining 9. --- ## Root Cause Groups - **5/14 failures — Event-value key schema mismatch (commit `d20ffcbf`)** (suites: bc, dualfollower, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha\[parallel\], oc) Commit `d20ffcbf` ("CNF-19588: CI test for clockClass recovery after cloud-event-proxy crash", merged 2026-07-03 on `main`) changed `test/pkg/event/event.go:742–751` to key `StoredEventValues` by `v.Resource` (ResourceAddress, e.g. `/cluster/node/.../CLOCK_REALTIME`) instead of `v.DataType` (`"notification"`, `"metric"`). For `os-clock-sync-state-change` events, all values share the same ResourceAddress, so only the last metric value survives in the map. The consumer in `test/conformance/parallel/ptp.go:413–415` still reads `values["notification"].(string)` and `values["metric"].(float64)` — both always return zero-values — so `wasLocked` is never set to `true` and the 5-minute timeout fires unconditionally at `ptp.go:393`. This is a **deterministic regression**, not a flake. The hardware IS achieving LOCKED state; the test simply cannot see it. - **5/14 failures — T-BC FSM stuck in FREERUN (DPLL event pipeline failure)** (suites: tbc) The T-BC state machine in `pkg/event/event_tbc.go` requires `inSyncConditionThreshold` and `toFreeRunThreshold` to be initialized via DPLL-sourced events before it can transition out of `PTP_FREERUN` (see `event_tbc.go:491–508`, `593–597`). On the tbc-profile nodes (e810 NICs with external PPS sync, `ts2phcOpts: "-s generic -a --ts2phc.rh_external_pps 1"`), DPLL event delivery appears broken in the `release-5.0` production image, leaving the FSM permanently in FREERUN and causing all five serial tests that gate on `PTP_LOCKED` to time out. The `convergeConfig` function (`event_tbc.go:657`) that maps ptp4l interface events to the ts2phc/DPLL config namespace is the suspected fragility point for dual-NIC e810 topologies. - **3/14 failures — T-GM GNSS lock not achieved; phc2sys blocked** (suites: tgm) The linuxptp-daemon intentionally delays phc2sys startup until `HandleDelayedPhc2sysStartup` (`daemon.go:2094–2131`) receives a ts2phc offset with `|offset| < 1 000 000 000 ns`. On GM node `cnfdf32`, ts2phc is running (its SIGTERM test passes) but never reports a sub-second offset, indicating GNSS synchronization was not achieved. Without GNSS lock, `updateGMState` (`event.go:376`) never reaches `PTP_LOCKED`, `ptp4l` never emits `CLOCK_CLASS_CHANGE 6`, the phc2sys metric stays at 0, and all three WPC GM assertions fail in turn. Hardware DPLL showing LOCKED (`dpll=1`) is not sufficient per `event_test.go:92–95`. - **1/14 failures — DualNICBCHA phc2sys failover timeout** (suites: dualnicbcha\[serial\]) The serial DualNICBCHA phc2sys failover test failed because phc2sys did not switch to the secondary interface within the 60-second test window after the primary was brought down. This may be a timing sensitivity or an infrastructure issue specific to the dualnicbcha topology. --- ## Cross-Suite Patterns | Root Cause | bc | dualfollower | dualnicbc | dualnicbcha | oc | tbc | tgm | Suite-specific? | |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---| | Event key regression (`d20ffcbf`) | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ | ✓ (parallel) | ✓ | — | — | No — all parallel clock-sync suites | | T-BC DPLL pipeline failure | — | — | — | — | — | ✓ (5 tests) | — | Yes — tbc only | | T-GM GNSS lock failure | — | — | — | — | — | — | ✓ (3 tests) | Yes — tgm only | | DualNICBCHA phc2sys failover | — | — | — | ✓ (serial) | — | — | — | Yes — dualnicbcha serial only | --- ## Actionable Recommendations 1. **Fix `test/pkg/event/event.go` key scheme for `os-clock-sync-state-change` events (resolves 5 failures).** In `createStoredEvent` lines 742–751, use a composite key such as `v.Resource + ":" + string(v.DataType)` so the `"notification"` and `"metric"` entries are distinct even when multiple values share the same ResourceAddress. Alternatively update `parallel/ptp.go:413–415` to iterate the map and match values by content rather than by fixed key name. This is the highest-leverage fix: one code change to a single file unblocks 5 out of 14 failures across 5 suites. 2. **Investigate DPLL event delivery regression in the `release-5.0` linuxptp-daemon image for e810 dual-NIC T-BC topologies (resolves 5 tbc failures).** Reproduce locally by deploying the `release-5.0` image on an e810 node with `ts2phcOpts: "-s generic -a --ts2phc.rh_external_pps 1"` and checking whether `inSyncConditionThreshold`/`toFreeRunThreshold` are ever populated in `event_tbc.go`. Focus on `convergeConfig` (`event_tbc.go:657`) and the DPLL-event-to-threshold mapping path (`dpll.go:757,759`). 3. **Diagnose GNSS signal / ts2phc configuration on GM node `cnfdf32` (resolves 3 tgm failures).** Check whether the GNSS antenna/cabling on `cnfdf32` is healthy, whether `ts2phc` logs show a sub-second lock, and whether any recent change to the `release-5.0` or `release-4.22` linuxptp-daemon image altered the `HandleDelayedPhc2sysStartup` offset threshold or the `updateGMState` GNSS-lock detection logic. If the issue is transient hardware GNSS outage, re-run the tgm suite to confirm; if reproducible, bisect the linuxptp-daemon image history against the last known-good tgm run. --- ## Analysis Summary | # | Test | Group | Classification | Confidence | Root Cause | Action | |---|------|-------|---------------|------------|-----------|--------| | 1 | `[bc-parallel] Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync` | Event key regression | test_regression | High | `d20ffcbf` changed StoredEventValues keys in `event.go`; consumer in `ptp.go:413` reads stale `"notification"` key | Fix `event.go` key scheme or update `ptp.go` consumer | | 2 | `[dualfollower-parallel] Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync` | Event key regression | test_regression | High | Same as #1 | Fix `event.go` key scheme or update `ptp.go` consumer | | 3 | `[dualnicbc-parallel] Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync` | Event key regression | test_regression | High | Same as #1; `inSyncConditionThreshold` stays 0; FSM stuck in FREERUN | Debug DPLL event pipeline on e810 dual-NIC nodes with `release-5.0` image | | 8 | `[tbc-serial] Downstream slave can sync to BC master` | T-BC DPLL pipeline | infra_other | Medium | Same as #7 | Same as #7 | | 9 | `[tbc-serial] Can provide a profile with higher priority` | T-BC DPLL pipeline | infra_other | Medium | Same as #7 | Same as #7 | | 10 | `[tbc-serial] Check GM announcements in locked mode and holdover transitions` | T-BC DPLL pipeline | infra_other | Medium | Same as #7 | Same as #7 | | 11 | `[tbc-serial]` (5th serial test) | T-BC DPLL pipeline | infra_other | Medium | Same as #7 | Same as #7 | | 12 | `[tgm-serial] Testing WPC T-GM holdover through connection loss` | T-GM GNSS lock failure | infra_other | High | ts2phc never reports sub-second offset on `cnfdf32`; `HandleDelayedPhc2sysStartup` never starts phc2sys; metric stays 0 | Check GNSS antenna/signal on `cnfdf32`; review ts2phc logs for offset convergence | | 13 | `[tgm-serial] ts2phc termination` | T-GM GNSS lock failure | infra_other | High | ptp4l never reaches clock class 6 due to GNSS not locked | Same as #12 | | 14 | `[tgm-serial] GNSS reboot` | T-GM GNSS lock failure | infra_other | High | Same as #13; 300 s timeout waiting for clock class 6 | Same as #12 | **Overall Issue Class:** test_regression (5 failures) + infra_other (9 failures); dominant actionable class is **test_regression** **Overall Severity:** HIGH — 5 failures are an immediately fixable deterministic test regression in `main`-branch test code; the remaining 9 are hardware/image-level sync failures requiring infrastructure and product investigation. --- # Per-Suite Details ## bc --- ### Suite: bc (1 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** The single `bc-parallel` "Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync" test failed because the OS clock never achieved LOCKED state within the configured 6-minute window, a pattern repeated across all five parallel event-based modes in the same job run. **Root Cause Groups:** - **Group 1: OS Clock Never Reached LOCKED State Within Test Duration** (affects: `[It] [bc-parallel] Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync`) - **Evidence:** - From `telco5g-ptp-tests__test_results.json`: `"[It] [bc-parallel] Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync": {"time": 363.773486739, "result": "error"}` - From `junit-failures.txt`: `Test: [It] [bc-parallel] Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync` / `Message: result: error` - The same `Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync` test fails with `result: error` in **all** parallel modes in the same run: `bc-parallel`, `dualfollower-parallel`, `dualnicbc-parallel`, `dualnicbcha-parallel`, `oc-parallel` (all recorded in `junit-failures.txt`) - Test ran for 363.77 seconds ≈ 6 minutes, matching the configured `slaveClockSyncTestSpec.Duration` timeout - CI build log confirms `USE_UPSTREAM=false` (`+ [[ false == \t\r\u\e ]]`), so production images (`linuxptp-daemon:4.22`, `cloud-event-proxy:4.22`) are from the nightly release payload `4.22.0-0.nightly-2026-07-27-080911`; all 6 nodes were `Ready` and the cluster setup step succeeded after `2h12m34s` - **Root Cause:** The test `testSyncState` (implemented in `test/conformance/parallel/ptp.go`) subscribes to `OsClockSyncStateChange` events via the consumer pod/cloud-event-proxy sidecar and waits until either the clock reports `LOCKED` state or the configured duration elapses. The 363-second execution time matches the 6-minute test duration timeout, hitting the path at `parallel/ptp.go`: `Fail("OS Clock was never LOCKED and test timed out")`. Because the identical failure appears simultaneously across all five parallel modes (bc, dualfollower, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha, oc), the root cause is systemic — either the PTP grandmaster/network was transiently unstable during the parallel test window, or the `cloud-event-proxy:4.22` image (from the 2026-07-27 nightly, incorporating PR `#722` "CNF-25885: Widen PTP clock thresholds when NTP failover is enabled" merged 2026-07-27) introduced a regression affecting event delivery or threshold logic. A backport of that commit to `release-4.22` could not be verified with the available evidence (`scripts/check_backport.py` was not present). - **Breaking PR/Commit:** Unknown — suspect `redhat-cne/cloud-event-proxy` commit `0a69e46` ("CNF-25885: Widen PTP clock thresholds when NTP failover is enabled", merged 2026-07-27) if backported to `release-4.22`; verify with `git log --oneline openshift/cloud-event-proxy release-4.22` - **Is it a flake?** Likely yes — simultaneous failure of the same test across all five independent parallel mode configurations in the same job run is characteristic of a shared transient hardware or network condition (grandmaster instability, GNSS signal dropout, or lab fabric blip) rather than a deterministic code regression. Retry the job to confirm. - **Suggested Fix:** Re-run the job to confirm flake; if it recurs, inspect `cloud-event-proxy` pod logs on the test node for error lines around `10:58` – `11:34` UTC and check whether commit `0a69e46` (NTP threshold widening) was cherry-picked onto the `release-4.22` branch, as loosened thresholds could prevent the clock from being classified LOCKED. **Suite Issue Class:** test_flake **Suite Severity:** MEDIUM ## dualfollower Based on all the evidence gathered, I have enough for the analysis. Here is the final output: --- ### Suite: dualfollower (1 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** The single parallel "Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync" test failed because the OS clock never reached LOCKED state within the configured soak-test window, after the PTP daemon was restarted during suite setup. **Root Cause Groups:** - **Group 1: OS Clock Never Reached LOCKED State After Daemon Restart** (affects: `[It] [dualfollower-parallel] Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync`) - **Evidence:** - From `investigation-summary.txt`: `Source: ptp.go:393, run.go:84` - `test/conformance/parallel/ptp.go:393`: `Fail("OS Clock was never LOCKED and test timed out")` - Test duration from `test_results.json`: `"[It] [dualfollower-parallel] Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync": {"time": 363.900288401, "result": "error"}` - Configured soak-test duration from `test/conformance/config/ptptestconfig.yaml`: `duration: 5` (minutes = 300 s); with `SynchronizedBeforeSuite` overhead of ~43 s and ~20 s per-test startup = 363 s total - From `parallel_suite_test.go:68`: `ptphelper.RestartPTPDaemon()` is called in `SynchronizedBeforeSuite` before tests begin - Same test failed simultaneously in all five parallel suites (bc, dualfollower, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha, oc) with timing of 363–364 s, consistent with all modes restarting the shared DaemonSet pods concurrently - Serial dualfollower clock-sync tests (`Dual follower can sync when one follower port goes down`, time=79s) **passed**, indicating the hardware itself is healthy; the daemon had sufficient time to lock by the serial phase (hours later) - From `ci-operator-build-log.txt:150`: `Step e2e-telco5g-ptp-telco5g-ptp-tests failed after 3h18m20s` - **Root Cause:** `testSyncState` in `test/conformance/parallel/ptp.go` calls `PushInitialEvent` (obtains the initial OsClockSyncStateChange — succeeds, showing the clock in FREERUN/HOLDOVER), then arms a `time.After(5 * time.Minute)` channel (line 362). All five modes call `ptphelper.RestartPTPDaemon()` concurrently in their `SynchronizedBeforeSuite` (line 68 of `parallel_suite_test.go`), causing repeated DaemonSet pod restarts on the shared test cluster. The dual-follower node requires more than the 5-minute window to re-establish phc2sys LOCKED state after this churn; when `tcEndChan` fires at 300 s (+ ~63 s setup overhead = 363 s wall time), the check at `ptp.go:392-393` fails: `if !wasLocked { Fail("OS Clock was never LOCKED and test timed out") }`. - **Breaking PR/Commit:** Unknown — no single commit introduced this; it is a pre-existing race between the soak-test window length and the daemon restart-to-lock convergence time, amplified by all five parallel suites calling `RestartPTPDaemon()` concurrently on the same 3-node cluster (cnfdf30/31/32). - **Is it a flake?** Yes — serial tests on the same run with the same hardware and images confirm the clock does achieve LOCKED state given sufficient time. The failure is reproducible under load (all modes restarting daemons simultaneously) but not deterministic. - **Suggested Fix:** Either (a) call `WaitForConsumerReady` / `WaitForPtpDaemonToBeReady` (which checks the health endpoint) in the parallel `SynchronizedBeforeSuite` **after** `RestartPTPDaemon()`, waiting until the clock reports LOCKED before starting the soak timer; or (b) increase `slave_clock_sync.spec.duration` in `ptptestconfig.yaml` from `5` to `10` minutes to give the dual-follower clock sufficient lock-convergence time after a daemon restart. **Suite Issue Class:** test_flake **Suite Severity:** MEDIUM ## dualnicbc --- ### Suite: dualnicbc (1 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** The single "Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync" failure is caused by a key-schema mismatch introduced in `test/pkg/event/event.go` on `main`: the event parser now stores values under ResourceAddress keys, but `parallel/ptp.go` still looks up the old DataType keys ("notification", "metric"), so the OS clock LOCKED state is never detected. **Root Cause Groups:** - **Group 1: StoredEventValues key schema mismatch breaks OS clock LOCKED detection** (affects: `[It] [dualnicbc-parallel] Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync`) - **Evidence:** ``` # Test received and processed the LOCKED event: [ptp.go: 411] got map[eventsource:/sync/sync-status/os-clock-sync-state-change eventtimestamp:2026-07-28 10:30:46.036513461 +0000 UTC eventtype:event.sync.sync-status.os-clock-sync-state-change eventvalues:map[/cluster/node/cnfdf30.telco5gran.eng.rdu2.redhat.com/CLOCK_REALTIME:0]] [ptp.go: 419] clockOffset=0.000000 # Test then timed out 5 minutes later: [FAILED] OS Clock was never LOCKED and test timed out In [It] at: /tmp/cnf-xjcAM/ptp-operator-conformance-test/test/conformance/parallel/ptp.go:393 @ 07/28/26 10:36:17.887 < Exit [It] PTP Slave Clock Sync ... (5m3.582s) ``` - **Root Cause:** Commit `d20ffcbf` ("CNF-19588: CI test for clockClass recovery after cloud-event-proxy crash", merged ~Jul 3 2026 on `main`) changed `test/pkg/event/event.go` to key `StoredEventValues` by `v.Resource` (the ResourceAddress, e.g. `/cluster/node/.../CLOCK_REALTIME`) instead of `v.DataType` (e.g. `"notification"`, `"metric"`). The consumer at `test/conformance/parallel/ptp.go:413-415` still reads `values["notification"].(string)` — a key that no longer exists — so `state` is always `""`, `wasLocked` is never set to `true`, and the 5-minute timeout at `ptp.go:393` fires unconditionally. The hardware IS locked (the OS clock sync-state event clearly carries `"value":"LOCKED"` and `clockOffset=14ns`); the test simply cannot see it. This same breakage affects all parallel "Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync" across bc, dualfollower, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha, and oc suites. The divergence in `event.go`: ```go // Before d20ffcbf: dataType := string(v.DataType) values[dataType] = v.Value // key = "notification" / "metric" // After d20ffcbf (current main): key := v.Resource // key = "/cluster/node/.../CLOCK_REALTIME" if key == "" { key = string(v.DataType) } values[key] = v.Value ``` The consumer in `parallel/ptp.go` was never updated to match: ```go values, _ := singleEvent[exports.EventValues].(exports.StoredEventValues) state, _ := values["notification"].(string) // always "", key doesn't exist anymore clockOffset, _ := values["metric"].(float64) // always 0.0 ``` - **Breaking PR/Commit:** `d20ffcbf` ("CNF-19588: CI test for clockClass recovery after cloud-event-proxy crash") — `main` branch only; **not** cherry-picked to `release-4.22`. Because the CI job clones test scripts from `main` (`TEST_BRANCH=main`), this `main`-only change is the effective root cause. - **Is it a flake?** No — this is a deterministic logic bug; every run with `main` test code against any event-based PTP clock will fail the same way. - **Suggested Fix:** Update `test/conformance/parallel/ptp.go:413-415` to extract the sync state from the ResourceAddress-keyed map (e.g., iterate `StoredEventValues` and find the entry whose key contains `CLOCK_REALTIME`, or add a helper that normalises back to DataType keys); alternatively,

The soak test (`testSyncState`) then opens a Ginkgo event subscription for `OsClockSyncStateChange` and waits up to the configured duration (≈6 min) for the clock to report LOCKED. After a daemon restart, the BC clock must re-lock and the consumer app + cloud-event-proxy must reconnect; if LOCKED state is not delivered via events within that window, the test fails at `ptp.go:393`. This is corroborated by the identical failure pattern in `dualfollower-parallel`, `dualnicbc-parallel`, `dualnicbcha-parallel`, and `oc-parallel` in the same run — a simultaneous event-framework delivery gap affecting all parallel suites. - **Contradiction resolved:** All bc-serial tests passed, including direct clock-sync checks ("Slave can sync to master", "BC clock class recovers to Locked after upstream link outage") and event tests ("Should check for ptp events", "Should receive events after linuxptp-daemon pod restart"). --- **TL;DR:** T-GM lock failure and ptp-operator reconciliation regression drove all 31 errors across 7 suites. **Overall Issue Class:** infra_other (primary); test_failure (secondary) **Overall Severity:** CRITICAL ## Root Causes (31 failures across 7 suites) ### 6/31 failures — T-GM "no solution found in Local GM mode" + systemic parallel Slave Clock Sync - **Suites:** bc, oc (direct); dualnicbc parallel (2 tests) | **Evidence:** PROVEN (bc, oc); HYPOTHESIS (cascade to dualnicbc parallel) - **What:** `testconfig.PtpConfigBC()` returned `"no solution found for BC configuration in Local GM mode"` in bc-serial BeforeAll (`testconfig.go:1458–1483`) and oc-serial BeforeEach; no PtpConfig CR was created, so downstream parallel "PTP Slave Clock Sync" timed out waiting for LOCKED (`parallel/ptp.go:393`). oc classifies as **infra_other** (GNSS signal loss on WPC T-GM hardware); bc classifies as **test_regression** (L2 discovery image v17 imposing stricter `StepSameNic`/`StepSameLan2` constraints). - **Breaking change:** oc: Unknown (infra). bc candidate (unverified): `70cecc0` (L2_DISCOVERY_IMAGE→v17, `testconfig.go:674`) - **Flake?** No — same topology-solver error across 7 suites in one run is infrastructure, not transient. - **Fix:** Confirm T-GM WPC node has GNSS lock (`ts2phc` reports LOCKED), then re-run; separately verify L2 image v17 resolves BC interface matching for this lab's wiring. ### 12/31 failures — DualFollower slave PtpConfig not reconciled by ptp-operator - **Suites:** dualfollower | **Evidence:** PROVEN - **What:** `test-slave1` PtpConfig existed but no ptp4l ran on cnfdf30; `PtpModeDiscovered=TGM`, `DiscoveredSlave1PtpConfig=nil`. GraphSolver confirmed physical DualFollower wiring (cnfdf32→cnfdf30→cnfdf32), so ptp-operator failed to reconcile DaemonSet onto the follower node. Daemon deletion during cleanup (09:18) triggered `discoveryFailure`, cascading nil-pointer panics at `ptp.go:2333,3544` in 9 subsequent tests. - **Breaking change:** Candidate (unverified): ptp-operator `release-4.22` HEAD at 2026-07-30 - **Flake?** No — deterministic reconciliation failure confirmed across the suite. - **Fix:** Investigate ptp-operator `release-4.22` reconciliation of multi-slave PtpConfigs; confirm `test-slave1` is accepted and applied, not silently rejected. ### 7/31 failures — T-BC multi-profile pmc cascade + missing downstream OC slave - **Suites:** tbc | **Evidence:** PROVEN - **What:** 1 test failed because `DiscoveredSlave1PtpConfig=nil` (no downstream OC slave in TBC lab topology). 6 tests failed via chain: `test-wpc-tbc` multi-profile warning triggered log-stream timeout (`183s`); pmc returned `bad command: GET` on wrong socket path (`pmc.go:17`); cloud-event-proxy crash test deleted all PtpConfigs, leaving `DiscoveredClockUnderTestPtpConfig=nil` (`testconfig.go:2093`) and panicking the final 4 tests. - **Breaking change:** Unknown (topology gap = lab provisioning; pmc path bug pre-existing in `linuxptp-daemon/pkg/pmc/pmc.go:17`) - **Flake?** No — deterministic given multi-profile TBC config and absent downstream slave. - **Fix:** Fix pmc socket path resolution for multi-profile T-BC configs; add downstream OC slave PtpConfig to TBC lab topology. ### 6/31 failures — linuxptp-daemon/operator restarts during active tests - **Suites:** dualnicbc (1), dualnicbcha (2), tgm (3) | **Evidence:** PROVEN (dualnicbcha); POSSIBLY FLAKE (tgm) - **What:** dualnicbc: `dialSocket()` logs ERROR on first reconnect after phc2sys SIGTERM (`daemon.go:136`), failing new `verifyProcessRestartNoSocketErrors` assertion. dualnicbcha: ptp-operator pod deleted 57s after `ens2f1` taken down, cycling all 4 linuxptp-daemon pods mid-test, leaving `PtpModeDiscovered=DualNICBC` (not DualNICBCHA) for parallel test. tgm: 3 WPC GNSS disruption tests failed in <0.4s in both dualfollower-serial and tgm-serial — BeforeEach LOCKED-state guard failure, consistent with residual GNSS transient state. - **Breaking change:** dualnicbc candidate (unverified): linuxptp-daemon PR #618 (`cf3c0f3`, Jun 30). dualnicbcha + tgm: Unknown. - **Flake?** dualnicbc: possibly (timing-dependent race). dualnicbcha: possibly (infra event). tgm: **yes** (transient GNSS state, retry recommended). - **Fix:** Demote `dialSocket()` first-attempt error to DEBUG in linuxptp-daemon; investigate why ptp-operator reconciles full DaemonSet rollout when HA primary interface goes down. ## Summary Table | # | Test | Suite | Group | Classification | Confidence | Root Cause (one line) | Fix | |---|------|-------|-------|---------------|------------|----------------------|-----| | 1 | Reboot discovery (serial) | bc | 1 | infra_other / test_regression | MEDIUM | No solution found in Local GM mode — T-GM not LOCKED | Confirm GNSS lock; oc classifies the identical error as `infra_other` (GNSS signal loss). Both cannot be correct simultaneously — if the T-GM was GNSS-locked, the topology solver would succeed regardless of image version; if it was FREERUN, no image would help. The oc interpretation (infra_other) is better supported by the breadth of failures (all 7 suites, 31 errors) and the determinism of the error. NOTE: Not overriding bc's classification, but the cross-suite weight of evidence favors `infra_other` as the dominant cause; L2 image v17 should be investigated separately after confirming GNSS health. 2. **bc serial-passed-but-parallel-failed:** bc CPU Utilization (parallel) **passed** while Slave Clock Sync (parallel) **failed**. No override needed. 3. **Unverified cascade — dualnicbc parallel to Group 1:** The dualnicbc analysis attributes parallel CPU + Slave Clock Sync failures to the systemic T-GM FREERUN, but dualnicbc's per-suite analysis calls these out separately. After phc2sys exits, `cmdRun` waits 1 s (`connectionRetryInterval`) then relaunches the process; `runSocketWriter` dials the cloud-event-proxy socket. If cloud-event-proxy is momentarily busy (old connection being torn down), `dialSocket()` at `daemon.go:136` logs at ERROR level and retries — but the log entry is already there, causing the test assertion `Expect(socketErrMatches).To(BeEmpty(), ...)` to fail. The release-4.22 linuxptp-daemon image includes PR #618 (Jun 30, OCPBUGS-94177), which redesigned phc2sys startup sequencing; combined with the new test added Jul 1, this transient dial failure is now surfaced as a hard failure. - Breaking PR/Commit: Test introduced by commit "Added tests to terminate processes and verify cleanup and restart without socket errors" (Jul 1, 2026, ptp-operator _(truncated — see per-suite analysis for full details)_ ### dualnicbcha --- ### Suite: dualnicbcha — 2 failures **TL;DR:** All linuxptp-daemon pods unexpectedly restarted during the phc2sys HA failover test, breaking both the serial log-watch and leaving the system in a degraded non-HA state for the parallel event test. **Suite Issue Class:** test_failure **Suite Severity:** HIGH **Root Cause Groups:** **Group 1: Daemon restart during phc2sys HA failover** (2 tests) - Tests: `[dualnicbcha-serial] PTP ClockSync DualNICBCHA phc2sys switches to secondary ptp4l when primary interface fails`, `[dualnicbcha-parallel] Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync` - Evidence: - Serial: `could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-f86rk container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=timedout waiting for matches` (ptp.go:1130 @ 09:53:05.818) - `DEBUG [collector.go: 530] Pod deleted: linuxptp-daemon-f86rk` and `Pod deleted: ptp-operator-77569bf778-rxkrp` at 09:53:00–09:53:05 (while test was watching for phc2sys switch) - Parallel: `DEBUG fullConfig=PtpModeDesired= DualNICBCHA, PtpModeDiscovered= DualNICBC` → `OS Clock was never LOCKED and test timed out` (parallel/ptp.go:393) - Root Cause: The serial test brought down primary interface `ens2f1` at 09:52:03 to trigger a phc2sys HA switch to `ens4f2`. --- **TL;DR:** T-GM node cnfdf32 stuck in FREERUN (ClockClass 248) caused 27/28 failures; 1 independent FIFO test regression. **Overall Issue Class:** infra_other **Overall Severity:** HIGH ## Root Causes (28 failures across 6 suites) ### 23/28 failures — T-GM FREERUN cascade (log-stream timeouts) - **Suites:** tgm, tbc, bc, dualfollower, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha | **Evidence:** PROVEN (tgm, tbc); HYPOTHESIS (bc, dualfollower, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha — unverified cascade) - **What:** cnfdf32 reported ClockClass 248 on ens7fx instead of ClockClass 7 (GNSS-LOCKED) for the entire ~4-hour run. Downstream daemons never entered sync, so `linuxptp-daemon-container` never emitted LOCKED profile lines; the test log-stream poller hit `timedout waiting for matches` / `context deadline exceeded` on every node across all suites. - **Breaking change:** Unknown — lab GNSS/ts2phc fault on cnfdf32. Candidate (unverified): linuxptp-daemon release-4.20 PR #629 (upstream sync 2026-07-22, commit `14eb788`) may have regressed ts2phc GNSS handling or log format. - **Flake?** No — ClockClass 248 persisted across all 6 parallel suites from first to last test. - **Fix:** Restore GNSS lock on cnfdf32, confirm ClockClass 7, then re-run. ### 4/28 failures — OS Clock never LOCKED (parallel sync tests) - **Suites:** bc, dualfollower, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha | **Evidence:** PROVEN (direct consequence of Group 1) - **What:** `Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync` in each suite's parallel phase timed out with `OS Clock was never LOCKED` — downstream effect of T-GM FREERUN; no independent root cause. - **Breaking change:** Unknown (same infra cause as Group 1) - **Flake?** No - **Fix:** Same as Group 1; no independent action needed. ### 1/28 failures — FIFO scheduling assertion mismatch - **Suites:** tbc | **Evidence:** PROVEN - **What:** `Should check whether using fifo scheduling` asserts `HaveLen(0)` but finds `{"test-wpc-tbc_tbc-tr": 10, "test-wpc-tbc_tbc-tt": 10}`. TBC testdata profiles (`profile-tbc-tr.yaml:156-157`) explicitly set `ptpSchedulingPolicy: SCHED_FIFO` / priority 10, so the assertion is stale or the profiles changed unexpectedly. `check_backport.py` exited with error (exit 2); cannot attribute to a specific commit. - **Breaking change:** Unknown — test assertion likely written against a prior profile state. - **Flake?** No — deterministic mismatch. - **Fix:** Confirm whether FIFO scheduling is intentional in TBC profiles; update `HaveLen(0)` or remove FIFO from profiles. ## Summary Table | # | Test | Suite | Group | Classification | Confidence | Root Cause (one line) | Fix | |---|------|-------|-------|---------------|------------|----------------------|-----| | 1 | Testing WPC T-GM holdover | tgm | 1 | infra_other | high | T-GM FREERUN; --- ## Cross-Suite Summary: periodic-ci-openshift-release-main-nightly-4.20-e2e-telco5g-ptp **Job:** `periodic-ci-openshift-release-main-nightly-4.20-e2e-telco5g-ptp` **Version:** 4.20 | **Total failures:** 26 | **Suites:** bc, dualfollower, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha, tbc, tgm --- **TL;DR:** GNSS-locked GM node `cnfdf32` never left FREERUN (ClockClass 248), cascading timeouts across all 6 suites. --- **Root Cause Groups:** - **25/26 failures — GM stuck in FREERUN (ClockClass 248), cascading to all downstream suites** Suites: tgm (3), bc (4), dualfollower (4), dualnicbc (4), dualnicbcha (4), tbc (6 of 7) The T-GM grandmaster node (`cnfdf32`, interface `ens7fx`) never achieved or re-acquired GNSS lock (ClockClass 7 / PRTC_LOCKED) during this run. All downstream BC and OC nodes depend on this GM. Because `ptp4l` / `ts2phc` on `cnfdf32` stayed at ClockClass 248 (FREERUN), no follower node ever emitted the expected `selected best master clock` or foreign-clock-ID log lines. Every test that polls `linuxptp-daemon-container` via a Kubernetes log-stream regex watcher timed out, producing either `timedout waiting for matches` (~205–237 s) or `error reading from stream: context deadline exceeded` (Kubernetes API killed the stream). The three tgm tests directly exercised GNSS manipulation (`ubxtool -z CFG-NAVSPG-INFIL_NCNOTHRS`, ts2phc kill/restart) and confirmed the GM never entered holdover; the metric `ens7fx/master:248` was reported instead of the expected `7.000000`. - **1/26 failure — TBC ptpconfig SCHED_FIFO / test-code mismatch** Suite: tbc (1 of 7) The TBC ptpconfig includes `SCHED_FIFO` scheduling for ptp4l/phc2sys processes, but the `main`-branch test (running against `release-4.20` images per `PTP_UNDER_TEST_BRANCH=release-4.20`) asserts that no real-time FIFO processes should be present. This is a suite-specific test/config skew independent of the GM state. --- **Cross-Suite Patterns:** | Suite | GM FREERUN cascade | Suite-specific | |---|---|---| | tgm | 3 (direct GNSS/holdover failures) | — | | bc | 4 | — | | dualfollower | 4 | — | | dualnicbc | 4 | — | | dualnicbcha | 4 | — | | tbc | 6 | 1 (SCHED_FIFO mismatch) | All suites share the same underlying GM FREERUN failure. The tgm suite is the **origin** (GNSS-layer failure on `cnfdf32`); bc, dualfollower, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha, and tbc are **downstream casualties**. Only tbc has one additional suite-specific failure unrelated to the GM state. The cross-suite daemon pod list (`linuxptp-daemon-4stqt`, `2gr6s`, `6xl5p`, `vfnqw`, `5lj7k`, `pb4kk`, `jmj9c`, `57h79`, `zqkqd`) spans every affected node, confirming this is cluster-wide, not a single pod crash. --- **Actionable Recommendations:** 1. **Retry the job immediately.** The `investigation-summary.txt` pre-classification is `Fix type: infra`. If the retry passes cleanly, this is a transient GNSS hardware event on the `cnfdf32` telco5g lab node. No code change is needed. 2. **Audit the GNSS receiver on `cnfdf32` around the run window (2026-07-28 ~02:07–05:30 UTC).** The tgm suite started at 02:07:08 and `checkStabilityOfWPCGMUsingMetrics` passed initially — the GM was locked at job start — but all three GNSS manipulation tests confirm it never re-acquired lock after the signal was disturbed. Check whether the WPC card's GNSS antenna feed, the `ts2phc` holdover duration setting, or the `ubxtool` threshold command left the receiver in a permanently degraded state for the remainder of the job. 3. **Fix the TBC SCHED_FIFO test/config skew.** Either update the `release-4.20` TBC ptpconfig to remove `SCHED_FIFO`, or update the `main`-branch test assertion to tolerate FIFO processes when the ptpconfig declares them. This 1/26 failure will persist independently of GM health. --- **Overall Issue Class:** `infra_timeout` **Overall Severity:** HIGH — all 6 test suites failed in a single nightly run; the E2E signal for OCP 4.20 telco5g PTP is completely dark for this build, masking any real regressions that may exist. --- # Per-Suite Details ## bc --- ### Suite: bc (4 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** All 4 bc failures stem from PTP synchronization never establishing on the lab nodes — the linuxptp-daemon log stream either closed early or never produced a master-clock-selected log line, and the parallel event test confirmed the OS clock never reached LOCKED state. **Root Cause Groups:** - **Group 1: linuxptp-daemon log stream unreachable / no sync log output** (affects: "Slave can sync to master", "Downstream slave can sync to BC master", "Can provide a profile with higher priority") - **Evidence:** ``` could not get any profile line, err=could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-4stqt container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=error reading from stream: context deadline exceeded ``` ``` could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-4stqt container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=timedout waiting for matches ``` From the CI build log, `ClockClassChangeIndicator = "selected best master clock"` (`linuxptp-daemon/pkg/daemon/daemon.go:65`) is the string the test regex hunts for in the streaming container log. Two distinct sub-errors appear: `context deadline exceeded` (the Kubernetes log-stream API call itself timed out) and `timedout waiting for matches` (stream was open but the daemon never emitted the expected log line within the test timeout of ~205–237 s). - **Root Cause:** The `linuxptp-daemon` (built from `release-4.20` images, `PTP_UNDER_TEST_BRANCH=release-4.20`) never achieved PTP synchronization with its upstream master during this run, so the log lines that encode the foreign clock ID (`GetForeignClockID` regex) were never written. The identical error pattern appears across every other suite in this same job (bc, dualfollower, dualnicbcha, tbc), all pointing at the same underlying hardware/network-level PTP failure rather than a code regression in any single suite. No image build errors were found in the build log; `USE_UPSTREAM=false` confirms production `release-4.20` images were used. - **Breaking PR/Commit:** Unknown — no code-level change is implicated; this is a cross-suite infrastructure failure. - **Is it a flake?** yes — the cross-suite pattern (every suite failing the same way in the same run, while infrastructure/nodes were Ready) is characteristic of a transient hardware or PTP network issue on the telco5g baremetal lab. - **Suggested Fix:** Retry the periodic job; if the pattern persists across multiple nightly runs, investigate PTP grandmaster hardware health and network connectivity on the `cnfdf3*` lab nodes. - **Group 2: OS clock never reached LOCKED state** (affects: "PTP Slave Clock Sync [dualnicbc-parallel]") - **Evidence:** ``` OS Clock was never LOCKED and test timed out ``` The same pod (`linuxptp-daemon-vfnqw`/`linuxptp-daemon-5lj7k`) that failed to produce foreign-clock-ID log output in Group 1 also failed to ever report an OS clock LOCKED event to the event-based parallel test. --- **TL;DR:** A test-code key collision in `createStoredEvent` (commit `d20ffcbf0c`, 2026-07-03) causes every "PTP Slave Clock Sync" event test to miss the LOCKED state and time out. --- **Root Cause Groups:** - **15/15 failures: Key collision in `createStoredEvent` silently drops LOCKED notification state, causing all PTP Slave Clock Sync tests to time out** — commit `d20ffcbf0c` on `k8snetworkplumbingwg/ptp-operator` main (merged 2026-07-03) changed the `StoredEventValues` map key from `v.DataType` to `v.Resource` (ResourceAddress). For `os-clock-sync-state-change` events, both the `notification` ("LOCKED") and `metric` (-2) DataValues share the same ResourceAddress (`/cluster/node/.../CLOCK_REALTIME`), so the metric entry silently overwrites the notification entry. `ptp.go:414` then reads `values["notification"]` as `""` (key absent), `wasLocked` never becomes `true`, and the test times out after 5 minutes. The clock hardware was confirmed LOCKED in raw CloudEvent data — this is a **test-code regression only**; production `release-4.22` images are unaffected. --- **Cross-Suite Patterns:** - **All suites share the same root cause** (`bc`, `oc`, and per the oc analysis also `dualfollower`, `dualnicbc`, `dualnicbcha`). The failure is systematic and code-level: every suite running `Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync` hits the same bad key lookup path in `testSyncState`. No suite-specific variation is present — the `clockOffset=0.000000` debug output (instead of the expected metric value) appears in both analyzed suites identically, confirming the key-mismatch affects both the `notification` and `metric` reads. --- **Actionable Recommendations:** 1. **Revert the map key change in `createStoredEvent`** (`test/pkg/event/event.go`, v2 path, ~line 730): restore `v.DataType` as the key for each DataValue loop iteration, undoing the `d20ffcbf0c` change. This directly fixes all 15 failures across every suite. 2. **Add a regression test for `createStoredEvent`** that asserts the returned map contains both `"notification"` and `"metric"` keys when given a dual-DataValue `os-clock-sync-state-change` event — preventing future key changes from silently breaking event parsing. 3. **Block the PR that introduced `d20ffcbf0c`** (or open a revert PR) against `k8snetworkplumbingwg/ptp-operator` main immediately, as every nightly run on this job will continue to fail 100% of the time until the fix lands. --- **Overall Issue Class:** `test_regression` **Overall Severity:** HIGH — all 15 test failures trace to a single deterministic code regression on main; no hardware or infra issues involved, but the entire nightly job is broken on every run until resolved. --- # Per-Suite Details ## bc --- ### Suite: bc (1 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** The single bc-parallel test "PTP Slave Clock Sync" timed out unable to detect a LOCKED OS clock because a test-code change in `k8snetworkplumbingwg/ptp-operator` commit `d20ffcbf0c` (July 3, 2026) introduced a key collision bug in `createStoredEvent` that silently drops the notification state from received CloudEvents. **Root Cause Groups:** - **Group 1: Key collision in `createStoredEvent` drops LOCKED notification state** (affects: `[It] [bc-parallel] Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync`) - **Evidence:** ``` [event.go: 730] Context Attributes, type: event.sync.sync-status.os-clock-sync-state-change source: /sync/sync-status/os-clock-sync-state Data, {"version":"1.0","values":[ {"ResourceAddress":"/cluster/node/cnfdf32.telco5gran.eng.rdu2.redhat.com/CLOCK_REALTIME","data_type":"notification","value_type":"enumeration","value":"LOCKED"}, {"ResourceAddress":"/cluster/node/cnfdf32.telco5gran.eng.rdu2.redhat.com/CLOCK_REALTIME","data_type":"metric","value_type":"decimal64.3","value":"-2"} ]} [event.go: 734] Latency for the event: 36942 ms [ptp.go: 411] got map[eventsource:/sync/sync-status/os-clock-sync-state ... eventvalues:map[/cluster/node/cnfdf32.telco5gran.eng.rdu2.redhat.com/CLOCK_REALTIME:-2]] [ptp.go: 419] clockOffset=0.000000 [ptp.go: 393] [FAILED] OS Clock was never LOCKED and test timed out ``` - **Root Cause:** Commit `d20ffcbf0c` on `k8snetworkplumbingwg/ptp-operator` (merged 2026-07-03, title: "CNF-19588: CI test for clockClass recovery after cloud-event-proxy crash") changed `createStoredEvent` in `test/pkg/event/event.go` (v2 path, line 730) to use `v.Resource` (ResourceAddress) as the `StoredEventValues` map key instead of `v.DataType`. This pattern is only emitted when the linuxptp-daemon calls `printNodeProfile(nodeProfile)` **after** `*configInput = configOutput` (`linuxptp-daemon/pkg/daemon/daemon.go:1152-1154`) writes the rendered config (containing `#profile: <name>`) back into `nodeProfile.Ptp4lConf`. Both the `*configInput = configOutput` assignment and the unconditional `printNodeProfile` call at line 1412 were **introduced in linuxptp-daemon `main` PR #617** (commit `fd6ff1e`, merged June 30 2026), but have **not been backported to `release-4.20`**. --- **TL;DR:** 11/13 failures are a test/daemon version mismatch; 2/13 are a T-GM hardware holdover regression. --- **Root Cause Groups:** - **11/13 failures — `Ptp4lConf: #profile:` log line absent from `release-4.20` daemon** (bc: 3, dualfollower: 3, dualnicbc: 3, dualnicbcha: 2) The test helper `GetProfileLogID` (test code running from `main`) searches for a log regex (`Ptp4lConf: #profile: <name>`) that is only emitted after `*configInput = configOutput` + `printNodeProfile` — code introduced in linuxptp-daemon `main` PR #617 (commit `fd6ff1e`, merged 2026-06-30) and **not backported to `release-4.20`**. The CI job deploys `release-4.20` daemon images (`USE_UPSTREAM=false`), so the pattern is never logged. `GetProfileLogID`'s internal 3-minute deadline consumes the entire outer `Eventually` window, leaving the `/var/run` fallback no time to run. All failures surface as either `timedout waiting for matches` (stream alive, pattern never appeared) or `context deadline exceeded` (Kubernetes log-stream dropped), with a secondary cluster-API `Unauthorized` state observed post-run in dualnicbc/dualnicbcha, consistent with infrastructure stress from 11 concurrent 200-second stream-read timeouts. - **2/13 failures — T-GM DPLL does not enter holdover on GNSS disruption** (tgm: 2) When GNSS signal is lost or rebooted on WPC T-GM hardware, the Intel DPLL is expected to transition to `lockStatus: holdover` (clock class 7). Instead, the DPLL stays in or falls directly to `FREERUN` (clock class 248), so `openshift_ptp_clock_class` never reaches 7. Both tgm tests fail: one by timeout polling for clock class 7, one by direct metric assertion finding 248. This is independent of the log-line issue; the `release-4.20` daemon or underlying firmware may have a regression in holdover state management. --- **Cross-Suite Patterns:** | Root Cause | Suites Affected | Failures | |---|---|---| | `#profile:` log line missing from `release-4.20` daemon | bc, dualfollower, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha | 11 | | T-GM DPLL does not enter holdover (clock class 7) | tgm | 2 | The 11-failure group is fully cross-suite: all four PTP topology suites hit identical pod-log-stream errors on six different pods across different nodes, ruling out any single pod, node, or suite-specific cause. The tgm failures are completely isolated to hardware GNSS/holdover behavior and share no mechanism with the other 11. --- **Actionable Recommendations:** 1. **Backport linuxptp-daemon `main` PR #617 to `release-4.20`** (fixes 11/13 failures). The commit `fd6ff1e` adds `*configInput = configOutput` and the unconditional `printNodeProfile` call that emits `Ptp4lConf: #profile:` into daemon logs. Without this, any `main` test branch that relies on `GetProfileLogID` will always time out against `release-4.20` images. Alternatively, gate the test's log-search path on daemon version and immediately fall through to the `/var/run` exec-based fallback instead of waiting a full 3 minutes first. 2. **Investigate T-GM DPLL holdover regression on `release-4.20`** (fixes 2/13 failures). Audit recent commits to `linuxptp-daemon/release-4.20` and `cloud-event-proxy/release-4.20` for changes in `pkg/event/event.go` DPLL state-transition logic. Also verify WPC hardware firmware version matches the expected holdover capability; if firmware is the variable, pin firmware version in the CI telco5g cluster. 3. **Extend the outer `Eventually` timeout in `BasicClockSyncCheck`** or make the `/var/run` fallback the *first* path (not the last resort). The current design serially exhausts a 3-minute log-stream deadline before trying a fast exec-based config read, meaning any transient log-streaming disruption (or version mismatch) guarantees test failure regardless of actual PTP sync state. --- **Overall Issue Class:** `test_regression` (The dominant failure mode — 11/13 — is caused by `main` test code depending on a daemon log line that exists only in `main`, not in the `release-4.20` images the job is actually testing. The tgm pair is `test_failure` / potential daemon regression, but does not change the overall classification.) **Overall Severity:** HIGH (11/13 failures are systemic and reproducible across every topology suite in every run that pairs `main` test scripts with `release-4.20` daemon images; the job cannot pass in this configuration without the backport or a test-side workaround.) --- # Per-Suite Details ## bc --- ### Suite: bc (3 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** All 3 BC ClockSync tests time out because `GetProfileLogID` cannot find the `Ptp4lConf: #profile:` log pattern in the `release-4.20` linuxptp-daemon container, and the `/var/run` fallback is exhausted within the 3-minute `Eventually` window. **Root Cause Groups:** - **Group 1: `GetProfileLogID` log pattern absent from `release-4.20` linuxptp-daemon** (affects: all 3 tests — "Slave can sync to master", "Downstream slave can sync to BC master", "Can provide a profile with higher priority") - **Evidence:** ``` could not get any profile line, err=could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-g59s2 container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=error reading from stream: context deadline exceeded ``` ``` could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-7khz9 container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=error reading from stream: context deadline exceeded ``` ``` could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-g59s2 container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=timedout waiting for matches ``` The same error occurs across **all four suites** (bc, dualfollower, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha) on different pods (`-g59s2`, `-7khz9`, `-68lc9`, `-d75dq`), confirming a daemon-wide, systematic failure. - **Root Cause:** `GetClockIDForeign` (called from `BasicClockSyncCheck` in `test/pkg/ptptesthelper/ptptesthelper.go:66`) first calls `GetProfileLogID` (`test/pkg/ptphelper/ptphelper.go:84`), which searches for the regex `(?m).*?Ptp4lConf: #profile: <name>(.|\n)*?message_tag \[(.*)\]` in the `linuxptp-daemon-container` logs. This pattern is only emitted when the linuxptp-daemon calls `printNodeProfile(nodeProfile)` **after** `*configInput = configOutput` (`linuxptp-daemon/pkg/daemon/daemon.go:1152-1154`) writes the rendered config (containing `#profile: <name>`) back into `nodeProfile.Ptp4lConf`. Both the `*configInput = configOutput` assignment and the unconditional `printNodeProfile` call at line 1412 were **introduced in linuxptp-daemon `main` PR #617** (commit `fd6ff1e`, merged June 30 2026), but have **not been backported to `release-4.20`**. The CI job runs test scripts from ptp-operator `main` against `release-4.20` production images, so the daemon never emits the expected log line. The `/var/run`-based fallback (`GetConfigForProfileFromVarRun`, added in ptp-operator `main` commit `84723c8` June 22) is invoked only after `GetProfileLogID`'s own 3-minute deadline elapses (~t=200s), which coincides with the outer `Eventually` timeout (3 min), leaving insufficient time to complete exec commands inside the pod and verify the result. `GetClockIDForeign` then returns `lastErr` from the log scan, and `Eventually` reports failure at ~202s. - **Breaking PR/Commit:** linuxptp-daemon `main` PR #617 (`fd6ff1e`, June 30 2026) added `*configInput = configOutput` and the unconditional `printNodeProfile` that the test requires — but this has not been cherry-picked to `release-4.20`. Per branch-awareness policy, a `main`-only change cannot be the root cause; the true underlying issue is that the `main` test code (`GetProfileLogID`, introduced in ptp-operator commit `73a75ec`, March 25 2026) was written assuming a log-output behavior that exists only on `main` linuxptp-daemon, not in `release-4.20` images. Secondary contributing commit: ptp-operator `bb43e35` (June 23 2026) has prerequisite `k8snetworkplumbingwg/linuxptp-daemon#215` which also affects daemon startup behavior

--- **TL;DR:** All 23 failures trace to T-GM node `cnfdf32` stuck in FREERUN (clockClass 248), collapsing PTP sync cluster-wide. **Root Cause Groups:** - **22/23 failures — T-GM GNSS loss / FREERUN (clockClass 248) cascading to all PTP consumers:** `cnfdf32` never achieved or held GNSS lock (node was only 24 minutes old at tgm suite start; DPLL went directly to FREERUN rather than HOLDOVER on signal loss). With no locked grandmaster, all downstream boundary clocks and slaves had no synchronization source. This manifested as: - Log-stream timeouts (`timedout waiting for matches`, `context deadline exceeded`) across dualfollower, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha, tbc — because `linuxptp-daemon` never emitted the sync-state lines tests poll for. - `OS Clock was never LOCKED and test timed out` in parallel event tests across four suites. - `Expected PMC clock class 6 … got 248` in tbc. - `Timed out waiting for clock class 7 / state FREERUN and ClockClass 248` in all 3 tgm serial tests. Affected pods: `linuxptp-daemon-qtq2t`, `k7dlc`, `2klsv`, `gvdmf`, `5zjch`, `htnss`, `49flz` — every daemon pod in the cluster. - **1/23 failures — Kubernetes API server degradation (dualfollower log-stream exhaustion variant):** By approximately 06:22 UTC the API server began returning `Unauthorized` / credential errors, causing `GetConfigForProfileFromVarRun` fallback exec calls to fail and the test runner process to be force-killed (`process timed out`, `exit status 127`). This is a secondary symptom of the extended infra instability in the same run. **Cross-Suite Patterns:** | Suite | Failures | Root Cause | |---|---|---| | dualfollower | 4/4 | FREERUN cascade + API server degradation | | dualnicbc | 4/4 | FREERUN cascade | | dualnicbcha | 4/4 | FREERUN cascade | | tbc | 7/8 | FREERUN cascade; 1 separate FIFO scheduling assertion | | tgm | 3/3 | GNSS/DPLL never entered HOLDOVER (root cause node) | All five suites share the identical FREERUN cascade. The tgm suite is unique in that it directly tests the T-GM hardware — the failures there are the _origin_, not a downstream symptom. The tbc suite has one anomalous failure (FIFO scheduling assertion mismatch) that is suite-specific and not shared with others. **Actionable Recommendations:** 1. **Add a GNSS lock readiness gate before tgm (and the full test phase):** Verify `ClockClass 6` and `nmea_status 1` stable for ≥5 minutes on `cnfdf32` before advancing past cluster setup. The 24-minute node age at test start is a clear signal the GNSS receiver had not converged. 2. **Retry the job with cnfdf32 at full lock stability:** This is a transient infra condition (GNSS signal loss / premature test start), not a code regression. No code change on `release-4.20` is implicated. A clean retry with a fully locked T-GM is expected to pass. 3. **Investigate lab GNSS antenna/RF chain on cnfdf32:** If retries repeatedly show FREERUN or short node ages, the antenna connection or signal source on `cnfdf32.telco5gran.eng.rdu2.redhat.com` (rdu2 lab) may have a hardware fault that needs physical inspection. **Overall Issue Class:** `test_flake` **Overall Severity:** HIGH --- # Per-Suite Details ## dualfollower --- ### Suite: dualfollower (4 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** All 4 failures stem from the test framework being unable to read expected log patterns from the `linuxptp-daemon-container` pod (`linuxptp-daemon-qtq2t`), a symptom present identically across every test suite in this job, indicating a cluster-wide API/log-streaming failure rather than any code regression. **Root Cause Groups:** - **Group 1: linuxptp-daemon log stream unreachable / log buffer rolled** (affects: all 3 serial ClockSync tests) - **Evidence:** ``` could not get any profile line, err=could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-qtq2t container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=error reading from stream: context deadline exceeded could not get any profile line, err=could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-qtq2t container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=timedout waiting for matches ``` Post-job gather steps confirm API degradation: ``` error: You must be logged in to the server (the server has asked for the client to provide credentials) error getting cluster version: Unauthorized ClusterVersion: Installing "" for <unknown>: <unknown> ``` The test entrypoint was force-killed: ``` {"error":"process timed out",...,"msg":"Process did not exit before 15s grace period"} error: failed to execute wrapped command: exit status 127 ``` - **Root Cause:** The test helper `GetProfileLogID` (`test/pkg/ptphelper/ptphelper.go:84`) streams the `linuxptp-daemon-container` logs looking for the regex `Ptp4lConf: #profile: <name>(.|\n)*?message_tag \[(.*)\]`. This line is only logged once at daemon startup via `printNodeProfile` → `printWhenNotNil(nodeProfile.Ptp4lConf, "Ptp4lConf")` (linuxptp-daemon `pkg/daemon/daemon.go:1521`). The daemon pods started ~2 hours before the dualfollower suite ran (cluster setup completed at 02:37, tests began at 04:13); ptp4l logs at ~1 line/second, so the 10 MB Kubernetes log buffer was fully rotated past the startup `Ptp4lConf:` entry by test time, causing the Pass 1 snapshot search to miss it. Pass 2 (live follow) then timed out (`timedout waiting for matches`) because the profile is never re-logged unless reconciled. The `GetConfigForProfileFromVarRun` fallback (`ptphelper.go:524`, exec into the container to inspect `/var/run`) also failed, consistent with the Kubernetes API server becoming `Unauthorized` / overloaded by ~06:22. The identical error pattern appears across every test suite and 7 distinct daemon pods (qtq2t, k7dlc, 2klsv, gvdmf, 5zjch, htnss, 49flz), which confirms the issue is cluster-wide, not profile-specific or suite-specific. - **Breaking PR/Commit:** Unknown — no code regression identified; the `printNodeProfile` log path in `daemon.go:1521` is unchanged in both `main` and `release-4.20`. - **Is it a flake?** No — the failure is consistent across every suite (dualfollower, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha, tbc, tgm) in the same job run, indicating a systemic cluster condition, not a random flake. - **Suggested Fix:** Retry the job; if it recurs, investigate whether the Kubernetes API server token expired during the 4-hour run or whether log buffer exhaustion is structural — in the latter case, consider adding periodic `ptpconfig` reconcile triggers or having `GetProfileLogID` issue an exec-based config read (`GetConfigForProfileFromVarRun`) earlier, without waiting the full 3-minute log-stream timeout. - **Group 2: OS Clock never LOCKED** (affects: `[dualfollower-parallel] Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync`) - **Evidence:** ``` OS Clock was never LOCKED and test timed out ``` - **Root Cause:** PTP clock synchronization requires the upstream ClockSync serial tests (Group 1) to first establish PTP sync state. Because the serial tests could not verify the PTP profile was applied (Group 1 failure), the clock never entered the `LOCKED` state, and the parallel event test timed out waiting for it. - **Breaking PR/Commit:** Unknown — cascading from Group 1. - **Is it a flake?** No — same cluster-wide condition as Group 1. - **Suggested Fix:** Same as Group 1; no independent fix needed for this test. **Suite Issue Class:** infra_other **Suite Severity:** HIGH ## dualnicbc --- ### Suite: dualnicbc (4 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** All 4 failures share a single root cause — the Telecom Grandmaster (T-GM) node was stuck in FREERUN (clock class 248, no GNSS lock), causing every downstream PTP clock in the lab to fail to sync, which made all log-scraping and LOCKED-state assertions time out. **Root Cause Groups:** - **Group 1: T-GM GNSS/hardware failure cascades to all PTP sync tests** (affects: all 4 dualnicbc failures) - **Evidence:** - From `junit-failures.txt` (dualnicbc serial tests, 3 occurrences): ``` could not get any profile line, err=could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-k7dlc container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=timedout waiting for matches ``` - From `junit-failures.txt` (dualnicbc parallel event test): ``` OS Clock was never LOCKED and test timed out ``` - From `junit-failures.txt` (tgm suite — the upstream hardware): ``` expected a metric [7.000000] but got map[/cluster/node/cnfdf32.telco5gran.eng.rdu2.redhat.com/ens7fx/master:248] ``` Clock class 248 = FREERUN (no GNSS signal); class 6 = GPS-locked. The T-GM node `cnfdf32` is not locked to any grandmaster source. - tgm suite: `Timed out waiting for clock class 7` (holdover) and `Timed out waiting for state FREERUN and ClockClass 248` — confirming the T-GM is unresponsive to GNSS-loss stimuli throughout the entire run. - From `ci-operator-build-log.txt`, all 6 nodes were `Ready` and the cluster was healthy, ruling out OCP infra: ``` [INFO] All 6 nodes are Ready. ``` - The same "timedout waiting for matches" / "never LOCKED" pattern appears identically across **all five** test suites (dualnicbc, dualfollower, dualnicbcha, tbc, tgm), confirming a lab-wide PTP sync failure, not a per-suite regression. - **Root Cause:** The T-GM hardware node `cnfdf32.telco5gran.eng.rdu2.redhat.com` (the GNSS-backed grandmaster) was running in FREERUN (clock class 248) for the entire test window — it was not receiving a valid GNSS signal. Without a locked T-GM, the boundary clock master (`test-bc-master1`, pod `linuxptp-daemon-k7dlc`) has no upstream PTP master to synchronize to, so `ptp4l` never outputs a foreign clock ID in its logs. The test framework (`GetProfileName`/`WaitForForeignMaster` in `test/conformance/serial/ptp.go`) polls the container log stream for that output with a ~202s timeout, finds nothing, and fails. The parallel event test's OS clock similarly never transitions from FREE_RUN to LOCKED. - **Breaking PR/Commit:** Unknown — the only recent linuxptp-daemon commit (`14eb788`, "Sync from upstream 22-Jul-2026") exists only on `main`, not on `release-4.20`. This job builds from `release-4.20` images (`PTP_UNDER_TEST_BRANCH=release-4.20`), so that commit cannot be the cause. No code change on `release-4.20` is implicated. - **Is it a flake?** Yes — transient GNSS signal loss or lab hardware instability at `cnfdf32` in the rdu2 telco lab. The `investigation-summary.txt` independently assessed this as severity LOW / infra class. - **Suggested Fix:** Retry the periodic job; if the T-GM comes back into GPS lock the run should pass. If this recurs across multiple nightly runs, investigate GNSS antenna/receiver health on `cnfdf32.telco5gran.eng.rdu2.redhat.com` and check whether `ts2phc` is able to read a valid NMEA/PPS signal. **Suite Issue Class:** infra_timeout **Suite Severity:** MEDIUM (all 4 tests fail, but cause is transient hardware/infra, not a code regression) ## dualnicbcha --- ### Suite: dualnicbcha (4 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** All 4 dualnicbcha tests fail because the PTP clock never achieved LOCK state — the `linuxptp-daemon` remained in FREERUN (clockClass 248), so every log-regex poll timed out and the event-based test saw no LOCKED signal. **Root Cause Groups:** - **Group 1: linuxptp-daemon never produces clock-sync log lines** (affects: `Slave can sync to master [Serial]`, `Downstream slave can sync to BC master [Serial]`, `DualNICBCHA phc2sys switches to secondary ptp4l when primary interface fails [Serial]`) - **Evidence:** ``` could not get any profile line, err=could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-gvdmf container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=error reading from stream: context deadline exceeded could not get any profile line, err=could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-5zjch container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=timedout waiting for matches ``` Cross-suite corroboration from `junit-failures.txt` (tbc suite): ``` Expected PMC clock class 6 for /var/run/ptp4l.0.config Expected <int>: 248 to equal <int>: 6 ``` And from the tgm suite: `expected a metric [7.000000] but got map[...master:248]` - **Root Cause:** The PTP chain never locks: `clockClass 248` (FREERUN) is reported across every suite in this run (dualfollower, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha, tbc, tgm all fail identically). The test framework polls `linuxptp-daemon-container` log streams for foreign-clock-ID regex patterns that only appear once ptp4l achieves sync; because the daemon stays in FREERUN those patterns are never emitted, so the 202 s timeout fires. The two error variants — `context deadline exceeded` (stream exhausted) vs. `timedout waiting for matches` (stream open, no match) — are both symptoms of the same absence of sync-state log lines. - **Breaking PR/Commit:** Unknown — no code change identified; failure is uniform across all suites and corresponds to clockClass=248 lab-wide, pointing to a transient PTP hardware/signal issue in the telco5g test bed (cnfdf30–32 nodes, rdu2.redhat.com). - **Is it a flake?** yes - **Suggested Fix:** Retry the job; if the same run-wide FREERUN pattern recurs, investigate GNSS signal and hardware connectivity on the cnfdf30/31/32 telco5g nodes. - **Group 2: Event-based OS Clock never LOCKED** (affects: `Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync [dualnicbcha-parallel]`) - **Evidence:** ``` OS Clock was never LOCKED and test timed out ``` (Identical messages in dualfollower-parallel, dualnicbc-parallel, tbc-parallel — all parallel event suites in the same job run.) - **Root Cause:** The event-based test waits for a `LOCKED` clock-state notification from cloud-event-proxy; because the underlying ptp4l/phc2sys never achieved lock (FREERUN across the board, same root cause as Group 1), no LOCKED event is ever published and the test times out. - **Breaking PR/Commit:** Unknown — same infra root cause as Group 1. - **Is it a flake?** yes - **Suggested Fix:** Same as Group 1 — retry once the test bed PTP signal is restored; the event-bus path is functioning correctly but has no synced clock to report. **Suite Issue Class:** infra_other **Suite Severity:** MEDIUM ## tbc --- ### Suite: tbc (8 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** 7 of 8 failures stem from the T-GM GNSS signal being lost (clockClass=248/FREERUN), collapsing PTP sync across all downstream nodes; the 8th is a separate test-assertion mismatch on FIFO scheduling. **Root Cause Groups:** - **Group 1: T-GM GNSS/infrastructure failure — PTP clock never locked** (affects: "PTP daemon apply match rule based on nodeLabel", "Slave can sync to master", "Downstream slave can sync to BC master", "Can provide a profile with higher priority", "Check GM announcements in locked mode and holdover transitions", "Should recover clockClass via event API after cloud-event-proxy crash", "PTP Slave Clock Sync [parallel]") - **Evidence:** ``` could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-htnss container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=error reading from stream: context deadline exceeded could not get any profile line, err=could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-htnss container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=timedout waiting for matches Expected PMC clock class 6 for /var/run/ptp4l.0.config Expected <int>: 248 to equal <int>: 6 OS Clock was never LOCKED and test timed out ``` From `junit-failures.txt`, the identical error (`timedout waiting for matches` / `context deadline exceeded`) appears in every other suite — dualfollower, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha — and from `test_results_tgm.xml`: ``` expected a metric [7.000000] but got map[ /cluster/node/cnfdf32.telco5gran.eng.rdu2.redhat.com/ens7fx/master:248] Timed out waiting for clock class 7 Timed out waiting for state FREERUN and ClockClass 248 ``` - **Root Cause:** The T-GM node (`cnfdf32`, the WPC Grand Master) never acquired GNSS lock — its ptp4l clockClass remained at 248 (holdover expired / FREERUN) instead of the expected 6 (LOCKED). With no locked T-GM, the entire downstream PTP chain (T-BC, T-BC-follower) had no source to synchronise to. As a result, `linuxptp-daemon-container` never emitted the log lines the tests poll for (slave-port selection, profile acquisition, clock-ID announcements), causing log-stream watchers to time out with `context deadline exceeded` or `timedout waiting for matches`. The clockClass-recovery test confirms the signal was never restored: `pmc` read 248 for the entire 300 s window. This failure pattern is identical across all five test suites in this job run, proving it is a cluster-wide hardware/environment condition and not a code regression. - **Breaking PR/Commit:** Unknown — no code change is responsible; this is a GNSS/RF hardware or connectivity failure on the test-bed node `cnfdf32`. - **Is it a flake?** Yes — this failure mode (GNSS signal loss causing full-suite collapse) recurs transiently when the test-bed loses GPS signal or antenna connectivity. - **Suggested Fix:** Retry the periodic job; if GNSS failures recur, the test-bed team should inspect the GPS antenna/receiver on `cnfdf32.telco5gran.eng.rdu2.redhat.com` and verify GNSS lock is stable before running PTP conformance tests. - **Group 2: FIFO-scheduling test assertion mismatch** (affects: "Should check whether using fifo scheduling") - **Evidence:** ``` Expected <map[string]int64 | len:2>: { "test-wpc-tbc_tbc-tr": 10, "test-wpc-tbc_tbc-tt": 10, } to have length 0 ``` From `linuxptp-daemon/pkg/hardwareconfig/testdata/tbc-gnrd.yaml`: ```yaml ptpSchedulingPolicy: SCHED_FIFO ptpSchedulingPriority: 10 ``` (present for both `tbc-tt` and `tbc-tr` profiles in the tbc-gnrd config) - **Root Cause:** The `release-4.20` deployed ptpconfig `test-wpc-tbc` has both `tbc-tr` and `tbc-tt` profiles configured with `ptpSchedulingPolicy: SCHED_FIFO` and `ptpSchedulingPriority: 10`. The test on the `main` branch asserts the active FIFO-process map should be empty (length 0), which contradicts the actual running configuration. **TL;DR:** A `main`-branch test key-naming regression breaks all 6 parallel event-sync tests; 4 tgm-serial failures stem from unported daemon features. --- **Root Cause Groups:** - **~6/16 failures — `createStoredEvent()` key-change regression breaks `testSyncState()` in all parallel suites** (`[bc-parallel]`, `[dualfollower-parallel]`, `[dualnicbc-parallel]`, `[dualnicbcha-parallel]`, `[oc-parallel]`, `[tbc-parallel]` — all: `Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync`) Commit `d20ffcb` (2026-07-03) / PR #711 commit `5e9ecf0` (2026-07-15) changed `createStoredEvent()` in `test/pkg/event/event.go` to store values under `v.Resource` (e.g. `/cluster/node/…/CLOCK_REALTIME`) instead of `v.DataType` (`"notification"`). `testSyncState()` in `test/conformance/parallel/ptp.go` still reads `values["notification"].(string)` — that key is now always absent. `state` is permanently `""`, `wasLocked` never flips, and every parallel event suite exhausts its configured window (~6 min) and fails deterministically at `ptp.go:393`. - **1/16 failures — ts2phc `process_restart_count` reset on PtpConfig delete/re-apply** (`[tgm-serial] Verify ts2phc metrics are cleaned up after GM PtpConfig delete and re-apply`) Commit `be1eb0d3` (2026-06-28) added a `main`-only CI test asserting the restart counter is monotonically non-decreasing across a delete/re-apply cycle. The linuxptp-daemon unconditionally deletes the `CounterVec` label set in `DeleteProcessStatusMetrics` (`cleanup.go:58`), resetting the counter to 1. The corresponding daemon fix does not exist in `release-4.22` or `main`, so the assertion fails every run. - **3/16 failures — T-GM ClockClass 7 holdover window too brief for detection** (`[tgm-serial]` "Testing WPC T-GM holdover through connection loss", "Continuously terminating ts2phc triggers FREERUN event and then recovers to LOCKED", "Testing T-GM Events on GNSS reboot and recovery") After GNSS disable, the hardware transitions through ClockClass 7 (holdover) transiently before reaching 248 (FREERUN). The polling interval and event-window size in the `main`-branch test code are too coarse to catch this transition against `release-4.22` images, which may move through holdover more quickly than assumed. By the time the test polls, ptp4l is already at class 248. - **~6/16 failures — origin unclear from provided analyses** (may be additional serial failures or unanalyzed suite variants) --- **Cross-Suite Patterns:** | Pattern | Suites affected | Suite-specific? | |---|---|---| | `values["notification"]` key mismatch | bc, dualfollower, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha, oc, tbc (all parallel) | No — cross-suite, same root cause | | ts2phc restart-count assertion | tgm only | Yes | | T-GM ClockClass 7 holdover window | tgm only | Yes | All 6 parallel suites share a single root cause (the key-change regression in `test/pkg/event/event.go`). Serial suites in bc, dualfollower, dualnicbc, oc passed their clock-sync tests — confirming PTP hardware sync was functional and only the event-reporting/test-parsing path was broken. The tgm failures are entirely independent and suite-specific. --- **Actionable Recommendations:** 1. **Fix `testSyncState()` in `test/conformance/parallel/ptp.go` (highest impact — unblocks ~6 failures):** Update the `values["notification"]` and `values["metric"]` lookups to use the ResourceAddress key emitted by `cloud-event-proxy` (e.g. look up by iterating the map or by a named constant matching the resource path format), or add a `GetValueByDataType(dataType)` helper in `test/pkg/event/event.go` so all consumers are decoupled from the raw key format. This single change restores all 6 parallel event-based suites. 2. **Gate the ts2phc restart-count test behind a daemon capability check or skip it on `release-4.22` (fixes 1 failure):** The test in `ptp.go:2628` was merged to `main` before the linuxptp-daemon fix exists. Either implement and cherry-pick the daemon-side counter-preservation fix to `release-4.22`, or add a version/feature guard so the assertion is skipped when running against images that do not support it. 3. **Extend T-GM ClockClass 7 detection polling in tgm serial tests (fixes 3 failures):** Increase the poll interval tolerance or use event-based detection (subscribing to `ClockClassChange` events) rather than log-polling to observe the holdover transition. The current test assumes a minimum holdover window that `release-4.22` images do not guarantee. --- **Overall Issue Class:** `test_regression` **Overall Severity:** HIGH — 10+ of 16 failures are deterministic regressions introduced by `main`-branch test code changes that were not paired with matching updates to all call sites or with the required daemon fixes; every run will reproduce these failures until the test code is corrected. --- # Per-Suite Details ## bc --- ### Suite: bc (1 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** The single bc-parallel failure is the "Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync" test timing out because the BC slave OS clock was never observed in LOCKED state within the 5-minute test window. **Root Cause Groups:** - **Group 1: BC slave OS clock never reached LOCKED state within test window** (affects: `[bc-parallel] Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync`) - **Evidence:** - From `investigation-summary.txt`: `Source: ptp.go:393, run.go:84` - From `junit-failures.txt`: `Test: [It] [bc-parallel] Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync — Message: result: error` - From `test/conformance/parallel/ptp.go:393`: `Fail("OS Clock was never LOCKED and test timed out")` - From `test/conformance/parallel/ptp.go:386–392` (tcEndChan branch): ```go case <-tcEndChan: if !wasLocked { Fail("OS Clock was never LOCKED and test timed out") } ``` - From `test/conformance/config/ptptestconfig.yaml`: `slave_clock_sync.spec.duration: 5` (5-minute window), `slave_clock_sync.spec.failure_threshold: 10` - From `ci-operator-build-log.txt`: `+ export PTP_UNDER_TEST_BRANCH=release-4.22` and `+ export TEST_BRANCH=main` — test scripts from `main`, production images from `release-4.22` - From `junit-failures.txt`: The **identical test fails in all 6 parallel suites simultaneously** — `[bc-parallel]`, `[dualfollower-parallel]`, `[dualnicbc-parallel]`, `[dualnicbcha-parallel]`, `[oc-parallel]`, `[tbc-parallel]` — all with `result: error`. Serial bc suite had `failures="0"` (78 tests passed). - **Root Cause:** `testSyncState()` in `test/conformance/parallel/ptp.go` subscribes to `OsClockSyncStateChange` events via the `cloud-event-proxy` sidecar, calls `event.PushInitialEvent()` (which succeeded — otherwise line 378 would have fired), then waits up to 5 minutes for any event carrying `state == "LOCKED"`. The fact that all 6 parallel suites fail the same event-based clock sync test simultaneously while all bc serial tests pass is the defining signal: this is a cross-mode systemic PTP environment instability, not a bc-specific code regression.
