Related context: OCPBUGS-60570 (event socket behavior, fixed in `4aa5310` on main). - **Is it a flake?** yes — The simultaneous failure of all 6 parallel `PTP Slave Clock Sync` tests while all serial bc tests pass is the hallmark of a transient PTP grandmaster or GPS-signal instability causing all downstream clocks to remain in FREERUN during the parallel test window. The `[tgm-serial]` test suite also shows failures for `WPC GM GNSS signal loss tests` and `ts2phc termination tests` (which intentionally disrupt the grandmaster), and the 5-minute test window is narrow enough that a temporary GPS/upstream disruption could prevent all parallel suites from observing a LOCKED transition. - **Suggested Fix:** Re-run the periodic job to confirm this is a transient flake. If it recurs consistently, check the `cloud-event-proxy` sidecar logs on `release-4.22` images for FREERUN/HOLDOVER reasons, and verify whether the GNSS/TGM disruption tests in `[tgm-serial]` are sharing timing infrastructure with the BC test nodes. **Suite Issue Class:** test_flake **Suite Severity:** MEDIUM ## dualfollower --- ### Suite: dualfollower (1 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** The single "Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync" failure is a deterministic test regression introduced by commit `d20ffcb` on `main` (July 3, 2026), which broke how PTP sync-state events are looked up in the test's key-value store, causing all parallel-mode suites to never detect a LOCKED clock state. **Root Cause Groups:** - **Group 1: `createStoredEvent` key-change regression breaks `values["notification"]` lookup** (affects: `[dualfollower-parallel] Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync`) - **Evidence:** - JUnit result: `"[It] [dualfollower-parallel] Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync": {"time": 363.898387443, "result": "error"}` — test ran its full ~6-minute configured duration before dying. - `investigation-summary.txt`: `Source: ptp.go:393` → which is `Fail("OS Clock was never LOCKED and test timed out")` in `test/conformance/parallel/ptp.go:393`. - Same test fails in **all** parallel suites (bc, dualfollower, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha, oc, tbc) in `junit-failures.txt`, confirming a systematic rather than per-mode cause. - From commit `d20ffcb` diff (`test/pkg/event/event.go`, lines 711–716 and 742–751 as shipped): ```go // Use ResourceAddress as key to avoid collisions… key := v.Resource // e.g. "/cluster/node1/ptp/CLOCK_REALTIME" if key == "" { key = string(v.DataType) } values[key] = v.Value ``` - `cloud-event-proxy/plugins/ptp_operator/metrics/manager.go:319–324` always sets `Resource: eventSource` (non-empty path like `/cluster/node1/ptp/CLOCK_REALTIME`) and `DataType: NOTIFICATION` for the sync-state value. - `test/conformance/parallel/ptp.go` `testSyncState()` reads: `state, _ := values["notification"].(string)` — this key is now **never populated** because the storage key is the resource path, not `"notification"`. `wasLocked` stays false for the full test duration. - CI build log confirms `TEST_BRANCH=main` — test scripts are from `main`, which includes `d20ffcb`. - **Root Cause:** Commit `d20ffcb` (July 3, 2026) refactored `createStoredEvent` in `test/pkg/event/event.go` to use `v.Resource` as the map key (to handle BC-mode multi-instance collisions), but the cloud-event-proxy always emits a non-empty `Resource` field (a full cluster resource path). The existing `testSyncState` in `test/conformance/parallel/ptp.go` still reads `values["notification"]` (the old `DataType`-based key), which is now empty for every event. Consequently, `state` is always `""`, `wasLocked` never flips to `true`, and the test exhausts its 6-minute window before failing at `ptp.go:393`. - **Breaking PR/Commit:** `d20ffcb` (`CNF-19588: CI test for clockClass recovery after cloud-event-proxy crash`) — merged `main` 2026-07-03. Not cherry-picked to `release-4.22`; this is purely a test-code regression on `main`. - **Is it a flake?** No — deterministic. The storage key change is unconditional; the lookup mismatch will reproduce on every run with this test binary. - **Suggested Fix:** In `testSyncState` (and any other consumer of `StoredEventValues`), replace `values["notification"]` with a lookup that iterates over the map looking for a value whose key ends in `CLOCK_REALTIME` (or reconstruct the expected resource path), OR revert the `createStoredEvent` key strategy for notification values to keep `string(v.DataType)` as the key and use a qualified key only for `METRIC` values where collision is the actual concern. **Suite Issue Class:** test_regression **Suite Severity:** HIGH ## dualnicbc Now I have enough to write the analysis. --- ### Suite: dualnicbc (1 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** The single dualnicbc failure is the parallel "Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync" test timing out after ~364 seconds because the OS clock never reported a LOCKED state event during the test window, a pattern shared identically across all six parallel event-based suites in this run. **Root Cause Groups:** - If `linuxptp-daemon#215` is present in the `release-4.22` image (delaying phc2sys startup until ptp4l syncs), the 1-minute log-search window may expire before phc2sys emits its interface-selection log. --- **Cross-Suite Patterns:** - **tgm-specific:** All 3 GNSS hardware failures are isolated to the tgm suite, which is the only suite running tests against real WPC T-GM hardware (cnfdf30–cnfdf32). No other suite is affected. - **bc-specific:** The nil-PtpConfig race is bc-serial-only; dualnicbcha and tgm do not exercise the `DiscoveredSlave1PtpConfig` discovery path. - **dualnicbcha-specific:** The phc2sys HA timing failure is isolated to the dualnicbcha suite and is a direct consequence of a recent `main`-branch test change not fully validated against the `release-4.22` production image. - **Common thread (bc + dualnicbcha):** Both non-GNSS failures stem from the `TEST_BRANCH=main` / `PTP_UNDER_TEST_BRANCH=release-4.22` split — test code on `main` is ahead of or not fully compatible with the `release-4.22` operator image, producing timing/discovery failures that would not appear if both branches matched. --- **Actionable Recommendations:** 1. **Fix the `DiscoveredSlave1PtpConfig` nil guard (bc suite):** In `test/pkg/ptptesthelper/ptptesthelper.go`, wrap the `DiscoveredSlave1PtpConfig` assignment site in an `Eventually` retry (analogous to the `3b93c9f` fix for clock ID lookups) so the test retries discovery if the slave PtpConfig is not yet reflected in the API server. 2. **Extend or retry the phc2sys log-search window (dualnicbcha suite):** In `ptp.go` around line 1098, replace the single `GetPodLogsRegex` call with an `Eventually` loop (e.g., 5 minutes, 10-second poll) consistent with how the preceding ptp4l SLAVE wait is structured, so the test tolerates variable delay between ptp4l synchronization and phc2sys log emission. 3. **Retry the tgm job to confirm transience:** The 3 GNSS failures show no code regression. `GetProfileLogID` (`ptphelper.go:69`) uses the regex `(?m).*?Ptp4lConf: #profile: %s(.|\n)*?message_tag \[(.*)\]`. This pattern is only logged by `printNodeProfile` (`daemon.go:893`) when `nodeProfile.Ptp4lConf` already contains the RENDERED config (i.e., after `*configInput = configOutput` at `daemon.go:1119` has updated the pointer's string on a PREVIOUS apply). On the very first profile application, `printNodeProfile` logs the RAW CRD config which has NO `#profile:` header and NO `message_tag` line — both are added only by `RenderPtp4lConf()` / `ExtendGlobalSection()`. Since the daemon applied profiles once at cluster-setup time (~2 hours before tests) and never re-applied them, neither Pass 1 (existing logs) nor Pass 2 (follow stream) in `GetPodLogsRegex` (`pods.go:214`) can find the pattern. Test 3 (higher priority profile) also creates a brand-new `temp` profile that is applied exactly once, so the same structural mismatch applies.
Full analysis
**TL;DR:** 13 of 15 failures are a single test flake — a log-rotation race that voids `GetProfileLogID`; 2 are a WPC T-GM holdover hardware failure. --- **Root Cause Groups:** - **13/15 failures — `GetProfileLogID` log-rotation timeout (bc, dualfollower, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha, oc):** `BasicClockSyncCheck` → `GetClockIDForeign` → `GetProfileLogID` (ptphelper.go:69) opens a `Follow: true` log stream and searches for `(?m).*?Ptp4lConf: #profile: <name>(.|\n)*?message_tag \[.*\]`. That regex only matches a line emitted once by `printNodeProfile` at daemon startup. Cluster setup finished at ~02:06; ClockSync tests ran at ~04:00–06:00 (~2–4 h later). By then CRI-O log rotation had cycled the startup entry out of the K8s-served log buffer. The snapshot read yields nothing (~26 s); the follow-stream times out after 180 s — producing the observed ~206–218 s failure duration across every suite. The "Can provide a profile with higher priority" variant adds ~60–90 s of profile-apply wait before hitting the same timeout (yielding ~287–298 s). - **2/15 failures — WPC T-GM DPLL holdover not sustained (tgm):** After GNSS disconnect, ts2phc either never emits the `"holdover"` log token (test 1 — 3+ min of retries, class stays at 6) or emits class-7 followed 7 ms later by class-248 (test 2 — DPLL flips through holdover and drops to FREERUN before the test can assert it). Root is in the WPC DPLL firmware or the `ts2phc_parser.go:162-163` holdover-detection logic, not in the test itself. --- **Cross-Suite Patterns:** | Suite | Failures | Root Cause | |---|---|---| | bc | 3 | Log rotation (GetProfileLogID) | | dualfollower | 3 | Log rotation (GetProfileLogID) | | dualnicbc | 3 | Log rotation (GetProfileLogID) | | dualnicbcha | 2 | Log rotation (GetProfileLogID) — same 206–218 s signature | | oc | 2 | Log rotation (GetProfileLogID) — same 206–218 s signature | | tgm | 2 | WPC T-GM holdover hardware/firmware failure | All five non-tgm suites share an identical root cause and near-identical failure durations. The dualnicbcha and oc per-suite analyses characterize the symptom as "LOCKED state timeout," but the timing signature (206–218 s, 287–298 s) and the `GetProfileLogID` call stack are identical to bc/dualfollower/dualnicbc. tgm is the only suite with a distinct, hardware-level root cause. --- **Actionable Recommendations:** 1. **Fix `GetProfileLogID` to not depend on rotated startup logs.** Instead of parsing the one-shot `printNodeProfile` startup line, query the current PTP configuration state directly — either from a `/metrics` endpoint, the `PtpConfig` CR status, or by triggering a daemon profile-reload (e.g., a no-op annotation update) immediately before the test assertion window. This single fix eliminates 13/15 failures. 2. **Investigate the WPC DPLL holdover firmware behavior on `cnfdf32.telco5gran.eng.rdu2.redhat.com`.** Capture `ts2phc` raw log output during GNSS disconnect to determine whether the DPLL hardware never enters holdover or enters and exits it within milliseconds. If the latter, check whether the DPLL holdover duration register is being set correctly from `ts2phc.holdover` config and whether a firmware update is available for the WPC card. 3. **Add a canary assertion for daemon log reachability before ClockSync tests.** A short pre-check that confirms `GetPodLogsRegex` can read at least one log line from the daemon container (not the specific startup pattern) would convert a silent 206 s timeout into a fast, labeled skip/infra-failure, making future regressions easier to triage. --- **Overall Issue Class:** `test_flake` (13/15); `infra_other` (2/15 tgm) **Overall Severity:** MEDIUM — the dominant failure mode is a pre-existing, deterministic time-dependent flake (not a code regression); the tgm failures indicate a real hardware/firmware problem on one lab node but affect only 2 tests. --- # Per-Suite Details ## bc --- ### Suite: bc (3 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** All 3 bc ClockSync tests fail at the same step — `GetProfileLogID` cannot find the regex `Ptp4lConf: #profile: <name>...message_tag [...]` in the linuxptp-daemon container logs, because this log pattern only appears on a second profile application and the daemon had been stable for ~2 hours since cluster setup with no re-apply. **Root Cause Groups:** - **Group 1: `GetProfileLogID` regex never matches — daemon log pattern requires second profile apply** (affects: all 3 tests) - **Evidence:** ``` [Jun 26 04:03:18.197][ptphelper.go: 123] GetProfileLogID attempt 1 failed for test-bc-master1_test-bc-master1: could not get any profile line, err=could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-nqmpf container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=error reading from stream: context deadline exceeded, retrying... [Jun 26 04:04:28.470][ptphelper.go: 123] GetProfileLogID attempt 2 failed for test-bc-master1_test-bc-master1: could not get any profile line, ...context deadline exceeded, retrying... [Jun 26 04:05:39.315][ptptesthelper.go: 68] GetClockIDForeign retry due to err: could not get any profile line, err=could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-nqmpf container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=error reading from stream: context deadline exceeded ``` Failure message (Test 1): `Timed out after 202.124s. Timeout to get foreign clock ID for ptpconfig test-bc-master1` Failure message (Test 2): `Timed out after 202.701s. Timeout to get foreign clock ID for ptpconfig test-slave1` (pod `linuxptp-daemon-2vqqd`) Failure message (Test 3): `Timed out after 201.746s. Timeout to get foreign clock ID for ptpconfig temp / err=timedout waiting for matches` Cluster setup completed at `02:06:48`; ClockSync tests started at `04:02:12` (~1h55m later). The daemon applied profiles ONCE at setup startup and was stable since then. - **Root Cause:** `GetProfileLogID` (`ptphelper.go:69`) uses the regex `(?m).*?Ptp4lConf: #profile: %s(.|\n)*?message_tag \[(.*)\]`. This pattern is only logged by `printNodeProfile` (`daemon.go:893`) when `nodeProfile.Ptp4lConf` already contains the RENDERED config (i.e., after `*configInput = configOutput` at `daemon.go:1119` has updated the pointer's string on a PREVIOUS apply). On the very first profile application, `printNodeProfile` logs the RAW CRD config which has NO `#profile:` header and NO `message_tag` line — both are added only by `RenderPtp4lConf()` / `ExtendGlobalSection()`. Since the daemon applied profiles once at cluster-setup time (~2 hours before tests) and never re-applied them, neither Pass 1 (existing logs) nor Pass 2 (follow stream) in `GetPodLogsRegex` (`pods.go:214`) can find the pattern. Test 3 (higher priority profile) also creates a brand-new `temp` profile that is applied exactly once, so the same structural mismatch applies. - **Breaking PR/Commit:** The structural flaw is in `GetProfileLogID` (`ptphelper.go:69`) itself — no single commit is the direct trigger. The two-pass `GetPodLogsRegex` was introduced/modified in commits `0f98a60` and `9c1bc7f`; `41f3dd9` (May 11 2026) changed the initial snapshot timeout from 3 min to 1 min but is not the root regression. The underlying issue — that `Ptp4lConf: #profile:` only appears in logs on the 2nd+ apply — has existed as a latent flake since `GetProfileLogID` was introduced. Unknown PR for the original introduction; the relevant release branch is `release-4.21` in `github.com/openshift/ptp-operator`. - **Is it a flake?** Yes — the test passes when something triggers a second profile application between cluster setup and the ClockSync test window (e.g., operator config reconciliation, hardware config event, daemon restart). In this run, ~2 hours of stability after setup meant no re-apply occurred. - **Suggested Fix:** In `GetProfileLogID`, instead of relying on the `Ptp4lConf:` log (which requires a second apply), read the ptp4l config file directly from the pod filesystem (`/var/run/ptp4l.0.config`)

--- ## Cross-Suite Summary: `periodic-ci-openshift-release-main-nightly-4.19-e2e-telco5g-ptp` **Job:** `periodic-ci-openshift-release-main-nightly-4.19-e2e-telco5g-ptp` **Version:** 4.19 | **Total Failures:** 22 --- **TL;DR:** 22 failures from three independent causes: cluster API server collapse, a linuxptp-daemon log-format mismatch, and a GNSS simulation code regression. --- **Root Cause Groups:** - **13/22 failures — Cluster API server VIP instability (`10.8.34.117:6443`):** - `bc` suite (11): 1 TCP i/o timeout on API VIP → 10 cascading `Unauthorized` failures as the kubeconfig bearer token expired ~2.5–3 h into the run with no reachable API server to refresh it. - `dualnicbcha` suite (2): log-stream connections to the same VIP terminated with `context deadline exceeded` when the cluster was still degraded. - All post-test artifact collectors (must-gather, `oc get` steps) also fully `Unauthorized`, confirming cluster-wide auth collapse by the end of the run. - **7/22 failures — `release-4.19` linuxptp-daemon missing `Ptp4lConf: #profile:` log / `/var/run` config header:** - `dualfollower` suite (3) and `dualnicbc` suite (4): `GetProfileLogID` exhausted its full ~202 s retry budget (`timedout waiting for matches`) for every tested profile name (`test-bc-master1`, `test-slave1`, `temp`). The `/var/run/ptp4l.*.config` fallback also failed, meaning the `release-4.19` daemon never wrote or logged the `#profile: <name>` header that the `main`-branch test framework requires. The `release-4.19` nightly image (cut `2026-06-25`) may have diverged from `main`'s `daemon.go:printNodeProfile()` or `config.go:RenderPtp4lConf()` behavior. - **2/22 failures — TGM GNSS simulation code regression (commit `7c915d9`, `main` branch):** - `tgm` suite (2): Commit `7c915d9` ("Replace ublox coldboot with config…") swapped out `ubxtool -p COLDBOOT` for `CFG-NAVSPG-INFIL_NCNOTHRS,50,1`. The u-blox module in time-only mode continues generating 1PPS even without a GNSS fix, so the Intel E810 DPLL never loses its reference and never enters HOLDOVER. Result: ClockClass 248 (FREERUN) instead of ClockClass 7 (HOLDOVER) — both tests fail (`Timed out waiting for clock class 7`; `expected metric [7.000000] but got 248`). --- **Cross-Suite Patterns:** | Root Cause | Suites Affected | Suite-Specific? | |---|---|---| | API server VIP instability | `bc`, `dualnicbcha` | No — same VIP `10.8.34.117:6443`, same run | | `#profile:` log/file missing (release-4.19 daemon) | `dualfollower`, `dualnicbc` | No — identical error string, different pods/nodes | | GNSS simulation regression (`7c915d9`) | `tgm` only | Yes — WPC T-GM hardware path only | - `dualfollower` and `dualnicbc` share an identical failure mode (stream alive, regex never matches) pointing to a `main`-test vs. `release-4.19`-daemon compatibility break. - `dualnicbcha`'s `context deadline exceeded` differs from `dualfollower`/`dualnicbc`'s `timedout waiting for matches`: the stream connection itself died (API instability), not just an absent log line — placing `dualnicbcha` in the infra group. - `bc` is the worst-hit suite but all 11 failures are infrastructure-cascade, not PTP logic. - `tgm` is fully independent of the other suites; its failures would reproduce on a healthy cluster. --- **Actionable Recommendations:** 1. **Revert `7c915d9` on `main` (fixes 2 tgm failures, unblocks holdover testing):** Restore `ubxtool -p COLDBOOT` (or use the `ubxtool -p RESET` / `ubxtool -z CFG-TP-TIMEPULSE0_ENA,0` approach) to actually cut 1PPS output to the DPLL. `CFG-NAVSPG-INFIL_NCNOTHRS` does not interrupt the hardware clock reference and cannot reliably trigger DPLL HOLDOVER on E810-based T-GM hardware. 2. **Investigate and remediate the API server VIP `10.8.34.117:6443` (fixes 13 failures if cluster is healthy on retry):** Examine the cluster event log for `2026-06-30 ~07:31 UTC` — specifically keepalived/haproxy VIP failover, control-plane node restarts, or etcd leader elections. The entire `bc` suite and `dualnicbcha` suite will pass on a stable cluster. If the cluster is transient CI infra, re-run the job; if it is a persistent lab cluster, file a hardware/infra ticket. 3. **Audit `release-4.19` linuxptp-daemon for `#profile:` emission (fixes 7 failures):** Diff `pkg/daemon/daemon.go` and `pkg/daemon/config.go` between `main` and `release-4.19` around `printNodeProfile()` (line ~819 in `release-4.19`) and `RenderPtp4lConf()` (config.go:437). If the `release-4.19` image no longer prepends `#profile: <name>` to rendered configs or no longer calls `printNodeProfile()` after config write, either backport the log statement to `release-4.19` or add a fallback detection path in the `main` test framework that does not depend on that header. --- **Overall Issue Class:** `infra_timeout` (dominant, 13/22) with a compounding `test_regression` (`7c915d9`, 2/22) and a probable `test_failure` / version-skew incompatibility (7/22) **Overall Severity:** CRITICAL — the entire job failed; root causes span infra instability, a committed code regression on `main`, and a potential `main`/`release-4.19` daemon compatibility break affecting 3 of 5 suites. --- # Per-Suite Details ## bc --- ### Suite: bc (11 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** The bc suite failed entirely due to infrastructure-level API server access loss mid-run — one test hit a TCP-level timeout reaching the cluster API VIP, and all 10 subsequent tests hit `Unauthorized` after the kubeconfig bearer token expired ~2.5–3 hours into the 3-hour job. **Root Cause Groups:** - **Group 1: API server VIP unreachable (TCP i/o timeout)** (affects: `PTP ClockSync Slave can sync to master`) - **Evidence:** ``` finding pod for test-bc-master1_test-bc-master1: Get "https://10.8.34.117:6443/api/v1/namespaces/openshift-ptp/pods?labelSelector=app%3Dlinuxptp-daemon": dial tcp 10.8.34.117:6443: i/o timeout ``` The test timed out after 617.575s. No code change is required on the ptp-operator `release-4.22` branch. **Suite Issue Class:** test_flake **Suite Severity:** MEDIUM ## dualnicbc --- ### Suite: dualnicbc (1 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** The single `[dualnicbc-parallel] Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync` failure is a cascade from the T-GM node (cnfdf32) being stuck in clock class 248 (FREERUN) due to GNSS signal unavailability, which prevented any downstream slave from ever reaching LOCKED state. **Root Cause Groups:** - **Group 1: T-GM GNSS signal loss / GM stuck in FREERUN (clock class 248)** (affects: `[dualnicbc-parallel] Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync`) - **Evidence:** ``` # From junit-failures.txt (tgm-serial suite, same CI run): Test: [It] [tgm-serial] PTP e2e tests WPC GM Events verification (V2) Testing T-GM Events on GNSS reboot and recovery [Serial] Message: expected a metric [7.000000] but got map[/cluster/node/cnfdf32.telco5gran.eng.rdu2.redhat.com/ens7fx/master:248] Test: [It] [tgm-serial] PTP e2e tests WPC GM GNSS signal loss tests Testing WPC T-GM holdover through connection loss [Serial] Message: Timed out waiting for clock class 7 # From junit-failures.txt (dualnicbc-parallel): Test: [It] [dualnicbc-parallel] Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync Message: OS Clock was never LOCKED and test timed out # From test/conformance/parallel/ptp.go:390-393: case <-tcEndChan: // The os clock never reach LOCKED status and the test has timed out if !wasLocked { Fail("OS Clock was never LOCKED and test timed out") } ``` - **Root Cause:** The T-GM node `cnfdf32.telco5gran.eng.rdu2.redhat.com` was reporting clock class 248 (FREERUN) instead of class 7 (PRTC/LOCKED), confirmed by the metric `map[/cluster/node/cnfdf32.../ens7fx/master:248]`. This is a GNSS signal availability failure on the physical testbed: without the GM achieving LOCKED state, no downstream PTP slave or BC node can synchronize, so `wasLocked` in `testSyncState()` (`test/conformance/parallel/ptp.go:387`) was never set to `true` before the test timer (`tcEndChan`) fired. All 6 parallel "PTP Slave Clock Sync" tests across all modes (bc, dualfollower, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha, oc, tbc) failed with the identical message, which is the characteristic pattern of an upstream GM failure cascading to all slaves. - **Breaking PR/Commit:** Unknown — no code change is implicated; the linuxptp-daemon `release-4.22` HEAD is `14eb78891b22cd2b7a4056fdac4f8131fb73649b` (Merge PR #629, upstream-sync-2026-07-22) and `check_backport.py` is not present in this workspace to verify. The failure pattern (GM FREERUN, GNSS timeout) points to physical testbed infra, not a code regression. - **Is it a flake?** yes — this is an infra flake: GNSS signal instability on the telco5g testbed hardware causes intermittent T-GM lock failures that are not reproducible by code changes alone. - **Suggested Fix:** Re-run the job; if the GNSS signal is restored the T-GM will reach class 7 and all downstream slave tests will pass. If recurring, investigate the GNSS antenna/receiver on `cnfdf32.telco5gran.eng.rdu2.redhat.com` for hardware or connectivity issues. **Suite Issue Class:** infra_timeout **Suite Severity:** MEDIUM ## dualnicbcha --- ### Suite: dualnicbcha (2 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** Both failures are caused by the OCP 4.22 `linuxptp-daemon` image now intentionally delaying phc2sys startup until ptp4l synchronizes, while the `release-4.22` test code was never updated to wait for that prerequisite — the fix exists only on `main` and has not been cherry-picked. **Root Cause Groups:** - **Group 1: Delayed phc2sys startup not accounted for in release-4.22 test code** (affects: `[dualnicbcha-serial] PTP ClockSync DualNICBCHA phc2sys switches to secondary ptp4l when primary interface fails`, `[dualnicbcha-parallel] Event based tests PTP Slave Clock Sync`) - **Evidence:** Serial test (`junit-failures.txt`): ``` could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-5qc8p container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=error reading from stream: context deadline exceeded ``` Parallel test (`junit-failures.txt`): ``` OS Clock was never LOCKED and test timed out ``` Build log confirming the deployed image is from `release-4.22`: ``` + export PTP_UNDER_TEST_BRANCH=release-4.22 + PTP_UNDER_TEST_BRANCH=release-4.22 ``` ``` git clone --single-branch --branch release-4.22 https://github.com/openshift/ptp-operator.git ``` `release-4.22` test code (`git show FETCH_HEAD:test/conformance/serial/ptp.go`): directly calls `GetPodLogsRegex` for phc2sys log immediately, **with no prior wait for ptp4l to reach SLAVE state**: ```go // Get phc2sys logs to identify which interface it's using const phc2sysLogPattern = `phc2sys(?m).*?:.* selecting (\w+) as out-of-domain source clock` logMatches, err := pods.GetPodLogsRegex(fullConfig.DiscoveredClockUnderTestPod.Namespace, ...) ``` `main` branch fix (`bb43e35`) commit message: ``` phc2sys is now intentionally delayed in linuxptp-daemon until the clock source (ptp4l or ts2phc) has synchronized. The DualNICBCHA test was timing out during the 1-minute log search window because phc2sys hadn't started yet. Prerequisite: k8snetworkplumbingwg/linuxptp-daemon#215 ``` Delayed startup confirmed in `linuxptp-daemon/pkg/daemon/daemon.go:923-926`: ```go if p.skipInitialStartup != "" { glog.Infof("Delaying %s startup: %s", p.name, p.skipInitialStartup) continue } ``` And cleared only after ptp4l reports sub-second offset (`daemon.go:2094-2131`, `HandleDelayedPhc2sysStartup`). Parallel test fail site (`test/conformance/parallel/ptp.go:393`): ```go // The os clock never reach LOCKED status and the test has timed out Fail("OS Clock was never LOCKED and test timed out") ``` Verified `bb43e35` and `402a5b0` are absent from `release-4.22` (30-deep `FETCH_HEAD` log: neither SHA nor their commit messages appear; `release-4.22` HEAD is `48030af`). - **Root Cause:** The OCP 4.22.0-0.nightly-2026-07-22-152558 linuxptp-daemon image includes the intentional delayed-phc2sys-startup feature (introduced by `k8snetworkplumbingwg/linuxptp-daemon#215`): phc2sys is held in `skipInitialStartup` state and only launched by `HandleDelayedPhc2sysStartup` once ptp4l reports an offset below 1 second (`daemon.go:2102`). The `release-4.22` test code at `test/conformance/serial/ptp.go` jumps directly to streaming phc2sys logs with a 1-minute timeout, so the context expires before phc2sys has emitted the `selecting … as out-of-domain source clock` line. For the parallel test, phc2sys not running means `CLOCK_REALTIME` never achieves the LOCKED state that `parallel/ptp.go:393` waits for. The fix (`bb43e35`, "test: update CI tests for delayed phc2sys startup") explicitly inserts an `Eventually` gate waiting for the primary BC slave interface to reach SLAVE state before searching phc2sys logs, and is merged to `main` but **not** cherry-picked to `release-4.22`. - **Breaking PR/Commit:** - Linuxptp-daemon behavioral change: `k8snetworkplumbingwg/linuxptp-daemon#215` (delayed phc2sys startup — synced into OCP 4.22 nightly) - Missing test fix (main only): https://github.com/openshift/ptp-operator/commit/bb43e35bc01d23cfbb2aa9bee59b4cae193c2021 — NOT cherry-picked to `release-4.22` - Related assertion fix (main only): https://github.com/openshift/ptp-operator/commit/402a5b07852f717809aad6e475323bc1907f0b2d — NOT cherry-picked to `release-4.22` - **Is it a flake?** No — the `release-4.22` test code has no mechanism to wait for phc2sys startup; every run against this image will deterministically time out until the fix is backported. - **Suggested Fix:** Cherry-pick `bb43e35` (and `402a5b0` for the related assertion fix) from `main` into `release-4.22` of `openshift/ptp-operator`. Alternatively, consider backporting `k8snetworkplumbingwg/linuxptp-daemon#215`'s context to `release-4.22` documentation so the relationship between daemon behavior and test expectations is explicit. **Suite Issue Class:** test_regression **Suite Severity:** HIGH

  • 4.22-e2e-telco5g-ptp (2026-07-25)
The test framework (`ptp-operator-conformance-test`) reads the pod log stream of `linuxptp-daemon-container` and matches regex patterns to extract a "foreign clock ID" and "profile line" — log output produced only when `ptp4l` achieves a master-slave PTP relationship and prints selection messages. Because `ptp4l` never successfully locked to a grandmaster in this run, those log lines never appeared, causing the 204–208 s per-test timeout to fire. The failure is not a code defect in the test regex itself but a genuine failure of PTP synchronization on the worker nodes; this is confirmed by the event-based parallel test below. -
Full analysis
--- ## Cross-Suite Summary — `periodic-ci-openshift-release-main-nightly-4.20-e2e-telco5g-ptp` **TL;DR:** All 26 failures caused by PTP grandmaster clock never locking on the telco5g lab cluster. --- **Root Cause Groups:** - **~22/26 failures — PTP grandmaster never achieved lock; serial log-stream tests timed out** Across every suite (bc, dualfollower, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha, and unanalyzed tbc/tgm), the test framework streamed logs from `linuxptp-daemon-container` and waited for a `ptp4l` foreign-master clock-ID line that never appeared. Because ptp4l never synchronized (same underlying cause as Group 1), no `LOCKED` event was ever published, and the test timed out. This is the direct, unambiguous confirmation that PTP hardware sync failed cluster-wide during this job. - **Breaking PR/Commit:** Unknown - **Is it a flake?** yes - **Suggested Fix:** Same as Group 1; additionally verify that `PtpEventsIsConsumerReady` is `true` before the sync tests run (the build log shows `PtpEventsIsConsumerReady= false` in at least one discovered config at `05:50:38`), and confirm the cloud-event-proxy sidecar container was healthy. **Supporting infrastructure context:** - Overall test pod hit the 3h45m prow wall-clock timeout (`Process did not finish before 3h45m0s timeout` at `2026-07-23T05:53:17Z`, killed with `exit status 127`). - Post-test gather step failed with `dial tcp 10.8.34.117:6443: i/o timeout` — the cluster API server became unreachable, indicating cluster-level instability by end of run. - `PtpEventsIsConsumerReady= false` logged at `05:50:38` confirms the event subsystem was not ready, which is consistent with a lab environment that never stabilized. **Suite Issue Class:** infra_timeout **Suite Severity:** MEDIUM (not CRITICAL — this is a known-flaky lab environment; no code regression detected; all basic operator health checks passed) ## dualfollower --- ### Suite: dualfollower (4 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** All 4 dualfollower tests failed because `linuxptp-daemon-45k98` never produced the expected ptp4l sync log lines, a systemic symptom shared across every suite in this job run, pointing to a hardware PTP infrastructure failure on the test cluster. **Root Cause Groups:** - **Group 1: ptp4l not producing sync log lines — log stream regex never matches** (affects: "Slave can sync to master", "Dual follower can sync when one follower port goes down", "Can provide a profile with higher priority") - **Evidence:** ``` could not get any profile line, err=could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-45k98 container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=error reading from stream: context deadline exceeded could not get any profile line, err=could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-45k98 container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=timedout waiting for matches ``` The identical error pattern appears for **every suite** in this job (bc, dualfollower, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha, tbc), each against a different daemon pod: ``` linuxptp-daemon-45k98 (dualfollower) linuxptp-daemon-47ghr (bc) linuxptp-daemon-mbltq (dualnicbc) linuxptp-daemon-fmc96 (tbc) ``` The Prow job also hit its global timeout: `"Process did not finish before 3h45m0s timeout"`. Two distinct failure modes appear: `context deadline exceeded` means the log API stream itself timed out (pod likely restarting), while `timedout waiting for matches` means the stream was open but ptp4l never emitted the expected pattern. Because this failure is cross-suite and cross-node, ptp4l was not achieving clock synchronization on any of the test nodes — the most likely cause is a failure of the shared hardware PTP grandmaster or network path on the `telco5gran.eng.rdu2.redhat.com` cluster, exacerbated by the cluster API server at `10.8.34.117` becoming unreachable during the run. - **Breaking PR/Commit:** Unknown — no code change is implicated; the deployed images built successfully from `release-4.20` and the cluster was fully provisioned (`Step e2e-telco5g-ptp-telco5g-ptp-cluster-setup succeeded after 2h3m41s`). - **Is it a flake?** yes — this is consistent with a transient hardware/infrastructure outage on the dedicated telco test cluster, not a reproducible code regression. Because ptp4l never synced to a grandmaster (same root cause as Group 1), phc2sys could not lock the system clock, causing every instance of this test across all suites to time out. - **Breaking PR/Commit:** Unknown — same infrastructure root cause as Group 1. - **Is it a flake?** yes - **Suggested Fix:** Same as Group 1; the OS-clock-LOCKED test is a downstream symptom of ptp4l sync failure. **Suite Issue Class:** infra_other **Suite Severity:** HIGH ## dualnicbc --- ### Suite: dualnicbc (4 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** All 4 failures share a single root cause — PTP clock synchronization never achieved lock, causing every test that streams `linuxptp-daemon-container` logs to time out waiting for a pattern that never appeared. **Root Cause Groups:** - **Group 1: linuxptp-daemon log stream never produces expected sync pattern** (affects: "Slave can sync to master", "Downstream slave can sync to BC master", "Can provide a profile with higher priority") - **Evidence:** ``` could not get any profile line, err=could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-mbltq container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=timedout waiting for matches ``` ``` could not get any profile line, err=could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-kxc27 container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=error reading from stream: context deadline exceeded ``` (junit-failures.txt lines 61–86; prompt.txt test error text) Additionally, the same "could not find regex in log stream … linuxptp-daemon-container" pattern is present verbatim across **all** other suites run in this job (bc, dualfollower, dualnicbcha, tbc — junit-failures.txt lines 1–141), confirming this is systemic and not isolated to dualnicbc. - **Root Cause:** The test helper (`GetForeignClockID` / `WaitForProfileFromLog` in the test framework) streams pod logs from `linuxptp-daemon-container` and regex-matches for a PTP synchronization line (foreign master clock ID or profile activation). The test helper (`ptphelper.go`, function `GetProfileLog`/`GetForeignClockID`) polls the pod log stream with a context deadline; since no matching lines are emitted, it exits with the deadline error. --- **TL;DR:** TBC T-BC discovery is broken (6/9 failures); holdover flakiness and a phc2sys HA timeout account for the rest. **Root Cause Groups:** - **6/9 failures — T-BC discovery broken (`GetInterfaces` reads only `Profile[0]`):** All 6 `tbc` suite tests fail because `api/v1/ptpconfig_webhook.go::GetInterfaces` unconditionally indexes `config.Spec.Profile[0]`, leaving `masterIfCount: 0` and `DiscoveredMasterInterfaces=[]` for the two-profile `test-wpc-tbc` PtpConfig. Introduced by ptp-operator PR #205 (merged 2026-06-23) exposing a pre-existing bug in the webhook. - **2/9 failures — WPC T-GM holdover not reached/sustained (flaky hardware on cnfdf32):** Both `tgm-serial` tests fail because the WPC T-GM on `cnfdf32.telco5gran.eng.rdu2.redhat.com` either never enters clock class 7 within 3 minutes, or flashes through class 7 so briefly the test cannot observe it before reverting to class 248 (FREERUN). Likely a near-zero `LocalHoldoverTimeout` or unreliable GNSS-disable simulation on this node's firmware. - **1/9 failures — phc2sys HA failover timeout (dualnicbcha):** `phc2sys` does not emit the secondary-interface selection log line within 60 s after `ens2f1` is taken down; `GetPodLogsRegexSince` hits `context deadline exceeded`. Not the same root cause. - **tgm is hardware-environment-specific:** The holdover failures are isolated to one physical node (`cnfdf32`) and do not overlap with the software-side failures in the other suites. **Actionable Recommendations:** 1. **Fix `GetInterfaces` in `api/v1/ptpconfig_webhook.go`** — iterate over all `config.Spec.Profile[:]` entries (not just `[0]`), aggregating master/slave interfaces by `ptpRole`. If the holdover window is hardware-limited, extend test polling budgets or skip on nodes known to have short holdover. 3. **Add a passing-baseline check for the phc2sys HA failover test** — instrument or log the time phc2sys takes to emit the secondary-interface selection after primary link-down, and compare against the 60 s timeout to determine whether the timeout needs extending or the `linuxptp-daemon` upstream sync introduced a regression in HA switching speed. **Overall Issue Class:** `test_regression` (primary: code bug in webhook exposed by new TBC tests) + `test_flake` (secondary: hardware holdover and timing sensitivity) **Overall Severity:** HIGH — 6 of 9 failures have a known, fixable root cause with a clear code change; Full analysis
--- **TL;DR:** A single test-code timeout regression blocks clock-sync validation in 20/22 tests; 2 remaining failures expose a known DPLL holdover gap. --- **Root Cause Groups:** - **20/22 failures — `GetProfileLogID` log-stream snapshot times out (test regression, commit `41f3dd9`):** Suites bc (3), dualfollower (3), dualnicbc (3), dualnicbcha (3), oc (2), tbc (6). Commit `41f3dd9bf0b33f642d931eb005e54eba89b337fa` ("address gemini review", Feb 26 2026) silently reduced the `GetPodLogsRegex` snapshot context from `TimeoutIn3Minutes` to `TimeoutIn1Minute`. By the time any suite runs (the `linuxptp-daemon` has been up for 3–5 hours), the container's accumulated log volume cannot be streamed within 1 minute; `io.ReadAll` returns `context deadline exceeded`. The fallback follow-stream also fails because the `Ptp4lConf: #profile:` marker was written only at daemon startup and does not reappear. All six affected suites produce the same `GetProfileLogID attempt N failed … error reading from stream: context deadline exceeded / timedout waiting for matches` chain before exhausting the 202-second test deadline. The tbc suite (6 failures) has a compounding factor: `linuxptp-daemon-t68bh` on `cnfdf32` is also crash-looping (suspected duplicate `DiscoveredFollowerInterfaces=[ens7f0 ens7f0]` config), which kills active streams mid-read and amplifies the timeout. - **2/22 failures — DPLL holdover collapses to FREERUN (class 248) without sustaining class 7 (known tech debt):** Suite tgm (2). In `linuxptp-daemon/pkg/dpll/dpll.go:656`, the `DPLL_HOLDOVER` branch immediately checks `!d.inSpec`; if `inSpec` was cleared during the preceding `DPLL_FREERUN` transition, the daemon fires `holdoverCloseCh` and falls to class 248 without ever holding class 7. The code carries an explicit `// TODO: GNSS holdover currently doesn't offer holdover out of spec` comment. Both TGM holdover tests fail deterministically on `cnfdf32`. --- **Cross-Suite Patterns:** | Root Cause | Suites affected | Suite-specific? | |---|---|---| | `GetProfileLogID` 1-min timeout regression | bc, dualfollower, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha, oc, tbc | No — all 6 suites hit identical error path | | tbc crash-loop on `cnfdf32` (compounding) | tbc only | Yes — specific to T-BC node config | | DPLL holdover tech debt | tgm only | Yes — only holdover tests exercise this path | All suites share the identical `ptptesthelper.go:71` → `ptphelper.go:123` → `pods.go:220` call chain. The `oc` suite provided the most complete trace, isolating `41f3dd9` as the breaking commit. The `dualnicbc` per-suite analysis incorrectly attributed the cause to hardware/GM unavailability; the actual mechanism is the same log-stream timeout seen everywhere else. --- **Actionable Recommendations:** 1. **Revert or fix commit `41f3dd9` in `GetPodLogsRegex` (highest impact — fixes 20/22 failures):** In `test/pkg/pods/pods.go` (around line 220), restore the snapshot context timeout from `TimeoutIn1Minute` back to `TimeoutIn3Minutes` (as set by the prior commit `9c1bc7f`). Alternatively, redesign `GetProfileLogID` to search only the log tail (e.g., `sinceSeconds` or `tailLines` parameter on the Kubernetes log request) rather than re-reading the full accumulated log, making the lookup O(recent output) regardless of daemon uptime. 2. **Investigate and fix the `linuxptp-daemon` crash-loop on `cnfdf32` (fixes tbc amplification):** The `DiscoveredFollowerInterfaces=[ens7f0 ens7f0]` duplicate entry in the T-BC config suggests a profile-discovery bug where the same interface is registered twice. Confirm whether `test-wpc-tbc` profile configuration is correct for node `cnfdf32.telco5gran.eng.rdu2.redhat.com`, and fix the `tbc-tr`/`tbc-tt` interface mapping to prevent the crash-loop that makes log-stream reads impossible even with a corrected timeout. 3. **Address the DPLL holdover tech debt in `linuxptp-daemon/pkg/dpll/dpll.go:656` (fixes tgm failures):** Implement the deferred holdover-in-spec window so that after GNSS loss the daemon sustains `ClockClass7` for the holdover duration before falling to class 248, mirroring the T-BC holdover behavior called out in the existing `TODO` comment. --- **Overall Issue Class:** `test_regression` (dominant — 20/22 failures caused by `41f3dd9` test-code change; tgm failures are `test_failure` against known gap; tbc crash-loop is `infra_other`) **Overall Severity:** HIGH — a single test-code commit broke clock-sync verification across every PTP topology for the entire job, producing 20 false-negative failures that mask any real regressions in the 4.21 PTP stack. --- # Per-Suite Details ## bc --- ### Suite: bc (3 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** All three ClockSync tests fail identically because `GetProfileLogID` cannot find the `Ptp4lConf: #profile:` log entry in the `linuxptp-daemon` pod's log stream, causing the master-clock-ID lookup to time out before sync can be verified. **Root Cause Groups:** - **Group 1: Profile log-ID lookup failure — regex mismatch/log rollover** (affects: all 3 failed tests) - **Evidence:** - Test 1 (`Slave can sync to master`, ptpconfig `test-bc-master1`): ``` [FAILED] Timed out after 202.066s. **TL;DR:** The CI job's build/deploy pipeline never installed ptp-operator, wiping out 13 tests before they started; 4 remaining failures are linuxptp-daemon behavioral regressions. --- **Root Cause Groups:** - **13/17 failures — ptp-operator never deployed to cluster (bc suite, all 13 bc-serial tests)** The `telco5g-ptp-tests` step wiped and re-created the `openshift-ptp` namespace, launched a privileged podman pod to build ptp-operator, linuxptp-daemon, and cloud-event-proxy from their upstream `main` branches, but the build log is truncated at line 496 (mid-template-substitution). The step ran for ~1h46m and the operator never appeared — no Deployment, no Pods, no `PtpOperatorConfig` CR. Every bc-serial test aborted within milliseconds of start (`NotFound` on `ptpoperatorconfigs`, `deployments/ptp-operator`, and ptp-operator pods). - **2/17 failures — DPLL holdover not maintained on T-GM node `cnfdf32` (tgm suite)** Test 1: GNSS coldboot never pushes DPLL out of `DPLL_LOCKED`; ClockClass stays at 6, never reaches 7. An explicit `TODO` at line 656 acknowledges this gap. Upstream sync commit `fd6ff1e` (PR #617, 2026-06-30) and/or cloud-event-proxy commit `4b49778` ("fix CurrentState aggregation", merged 2026-07-06 ~1.5h before the job) are the prime suspects. - **1/17 failure — phc2sys HA failover silent within 60 s (dualnicbcha suite)** After `ens2f1` was brought down, no `selecting <iface> as out-of-domain source clock` log line appeared within the 60-second context deadline. The stream was readable (initial match succeeded), so this is a behavioral regression or a tight timeout. The `release-4.22` linuxptp-daemon image (same upstream sync commit `fd6ff1e`) is the runtime under test. - **1/17 failure — Prometheus unreachable during CPU utilization soak test (dualnicbcha suite)** An infrastructure-level failure independent of code changes; Prometheus was not accessible on the test cluster at that point in the run. --- **Cross-Suite Patterns:** | Pattern | Suites affected | |---|---| | Build/deploy pipeline failure (operator never installed) | **bc** only — cascaded to all 13 bc-serial tests | | linuxptp-daemon `release-4.22` behavioral regression (upstream sync `fd6ff1e`) | **tgm** (2 tests, DPLL holdover) + **dualnicbcha** (1 test, phc2sys HA) | | Infrastructure / observability failure (Prometheus down) | **dualnicbcha** only | The tgm and dualnicbcha phc2sys failures share a common suspect: linuxptp-daemon upstream sync commit `fd6ff1e` (PR #617). The conformance tests open a Kubernetes log stream to the `linuxptp-daemon-container` and look for ptp4l lines containing the foreign-clock-ID (the grandmaster clock identity that ptp4l emits once it reaches SLAVE state, e.g. `FOREIGN_MASTER_RECORD_SET` or similar). When ptp4l is NOT synchronized — because the upstream PTP grandmaster is unavailable or the sync chain broke — those log lines are never produced and the test times out. The fact that every ClockSync test on every suite failed simultaneously against multiple different pods on multiple nodes points to a cluster-wide PTP environment failure (grandmaster or uplink down), not an isolated daemon crash. Configuration/API tests in the same suite (operator deployment, lease, namespace) all passed, confirming the daemon pods were running; only the PTP-sync-dependent tests failed.
Full analysis
**TL;DR:** T-GM GNSS lock loss (ClockClass 248/Freerun) collapsed the entire PTP sync chain, causing all 14 failures across all 5 suites. --- **Root Cause Groups:** - **12/14 failures — T-GM GNSS unlocked (ClockClass 248), cascading to all BC/slave ClockSync tests** (suites: bc ×3, dualfollower ×3, dualnicbc ×3, dualnicbcha ×2, tgm ×2): The WPC T-GM node in `telco5gran.eng.rdu2.redhat.com` lost GNSS lock and, critically, failed to enter holdover (ClockClass 7) — instead transitioning directly to Freerun (ClockClass 248). This is confirmed directly by the tgm suite ("expected a metric [7.000000] but got map[metric:248]", "Timed out waiting for clock class 7") and is the upstream cause of all ClockSync timeouts in every other suite: without a GNSS-locked grandmaster, ptp4l on BC/slave nodes never reaches SLAVE state and the `GetForeignClockID` log-stream helper never matches its regex, firing the 202-second timeout on every test. The same `could not get any profile line / could not find regex in log stream` error message appears across 6 different daemon pods (`jvf75`, `9ssfb`, `z7mfj`, `lkhnx`, `s8frf`, `cklkh`, `h4bbm`) on multiple nodes. - **1/14 failures — Prometheus not returning valid responses during CPU Utilization test** (suite: bc ×1): The parallel CPU Utilization test in `bc` failed because Prometheus was returning no valid data. This is a secondary symptom of the same cluster-health degradation: a lab with a broken PTP grandmaster and, in some suites, API server credential expiry (`Unauthorized` at `07:53:36Z`) is consistent with a broader cluster instability window on 2026-07-03. - **Contributing factor (not a separate root cause) — Kubernetes API server degradation mid-run**: Some suites saw `error reading from stream: context deadline exceeded` (dualnicbcha ×2) rather than the log-stream `timedout waiting for matches` pattern (bc, dualfollower, dualnicbc). The API server also emitted `Unauthorized` errors at job teardown. This is a compounding instability but does not change the root cause — the PTP tests would have failed regardless due to the absent grandmaster signal. --- **Cross-Suite Patterns:** | Suite | Failures | Root cause | Suite-specific? | |---|---|---|---| | bc | 3 ClockSync + 1 CPU-util | GNSS loss (ClockSync); Prometheus degraded (CPU-util) | No — shared GNSS root | | dualfollower | 3 ClockSync | GNSS loss | No | | dualnicbc | 3 ClockSync | GNSS loss (most direct confirmation via tgm cross-reference) | No | | dualnicbcha | 2 ClockSync | GNSS loss + API server dropping streams | No | | tgm | 2 holdover/event tests | GNSS loss / DPLL not entering holdover | Primary source — not cascading from another suite | All 5 suites share a single upstream root cause. No failure is suite-specific. The tgm suite is the causal origin (DPLL hardware or GNSS disable behavior); all other suites are downstream victims. --- **Actionable Recommendations:** 1. **Immediately: Verify and restore GNSS lock on the T-GM node.** SSH to the `cnfdf30/cnfdf31/cnfdf32.telco5gran.eng.rdu2.redhat.com` T-GM node, check `linuxptp-daemon` logs for GNSS lock status, and confirm the DPLL reports ClockClass 7 before re-triggering the nightly job. Check GNSS antenna connectivity and signal health (GPS signal availability at the lab site). 2. **Investigate DPLL holdover behavior on GNSS loss.** The tgm evidence shows the WPC NIC's DPLL transitions directly from LOCKED → FREERUN (ClockClass 248) without an intermediate HOLDOVER (ClockClass 7) period. This may be caused by the `ubxtool CFG-NAVSPG-INFIL_NCNOTHRS=50` test-disabling mechanism producing an abrupt GNSS cutoff, or the hardware DPLL holdover timeout being shorter than the test's 3-minute polling window. Verify the holdover duration configuration on the WPC NIC and consider whether the test should wait longer or use a softer GNSS-disable method. 3. **Investigate API server token TTL relative to job duration.** The job runs for ~2h50m and the API server was issuing `Unauthorized` errors at teardown. If the CI kubeconfig token has a TTL shorter than the job duration, all long-running nightly jobs will degrade. Rotate or extend the credential lifetime in the `telco5gran` lab CI configuration. --- **Overall Issue Class:** `infra_other` (hardware GNSS failure in the test lab, not a code regression or transient flake) **Overall Severity:** HIGH — 14/14 failures in a nightly periodic job, all suites impacted, no code changes implicated. The job is completely blocked until GNSS lock is restored, but there is no OCP 4.20 code defect to fix. --- # Per-Suite Details ## bc --- ### Suite: bc (4 failures) **Suite TL;DR:** All 3 ClockSync serial tests timed out unable to read the foreign-clock-ID from `linuxptp-daemon-container` pod logs, and the parallel CPU Utilization test failed because Prometheus was not returning valid responses — both symptoms of a systemic infrastructure failure on the test cluster on 2026-07-03. **Root Cause Groups:** - **Group 1: linuxptp-daemon log stream returns no foreign-clock-ID output** (affects: "Slave can sync to master", "Downstream slave can sync to BC master", "Can provide a profile with higher priority") - **Evidence:** ``` could not get any profile line, err=could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-jvf75 container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=error reading from stream: context deadline exceeded could not get any profile line, err=could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-9ssfb container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=error reading from stream: context deadline exceeded could not get any profile line, err=could not find regex in log stream for openshift-ptp/linuxptp-daemon-jvf75 container=linuxptp-daemon-container, err=timedout waiting for matches ``` All three failures occur against different daemon pods (`jvf75` for test-bc-master1/temp, `9ssfb` for test-slave1), confirming the problem spans multiple nodes. The same `could not find regex in log stream` pattern with identical semantics appears in every other suite that ran on the same cluster (dualfollower, dualnicbc, dualnicbcha) — 9+ failures total with the same root. - **Root Cause:** The conformance tests open a Kubernetes log stream to the `linuxptp-daemon-container` and look for ptp4l lines containing the foreign-clock-ID (the grandmaster clock identity that ptp4l emits once it reaches SLAVE state, e.g. `FOREIGN_MASTER_RECORD_SET` or similar). When ptp4l is NOT synchronized — because the upstream PTP grandmaster is unavailable or the sync chain broke — those log lines are never produced and the test times out. The fact that every ClockSync test on every suite failed simultaneously against multiple different pods on multiple nodes points to a cluster-wide PTP environment failure (grandmaster or uplink down), not an isolated daemon crash. Configuration/API tests in the same suite (operator deployment, lease, namespace) all passed, confirming the daemon pods were running; only the PTP-sync-dependent tests failed. The only candidate code change in the window is `openshift/linuxptp-daemon` commit `b022e1d` (OCPBUGS-95610, "Send ptp4l event when running without proxy", merged 2026-07-02, one day before the run). Inspection of the pre-OCPBUGS-95610 release-4.20 source (`3c31e2f`) shows that this commit adds `tBCTransitionCheck` only in the `!stdoutToSocket` branch (lines 1059-1082 of daemon.go) — the path taken when the cloud-event-proxy (CEP) sidecar is absent. The CI job deploys CEP (`SIDECAR_IMG=...cloud-event-proxy:4.20`), so `stdoutToSocket=true` and the `!stdoutToSocket` block is bypassed entirely. OCPBUGS-95610 is therefore not in the hot path for this job and is ruled out as the cause of these failures. - **Breaking PR/Commit:** Unknown — no code change found on `release-4.20` that would affect the `stdoutToSocket=true` (with-CEP) ptp4l sync path.
